===== DOCUMENTATION =====

===== DOC docs/index.md: Documentation =====
[image: cthangover logo]


Welcome to the cthangover documentation. Here you'll find guides, code examples, and configuration references for mod and core development.

## Kernel, engine mods and games

cthangover is a game engine, not a game of one genre. The C# kernel (`Core/`) is a **mod player**: it loads, compiles and
registers mods, resolves their data, keeps saves, runs the event bus and hosts the UI. It ships no game, no scene and no
genre — a visual novel and a top-down RPG are both just sets of mods.

**Engine mods** (`*_base`) are the building blocks a game picks: the 2D shell (`shell2d_base`), dialogues with the
scenario DSL (`dialog_base`), characters, items, quests, skills, the world map, time and lighting, the turn-based battle
platform with its cores (`card_battle`, `ff_battle`), the tile world (`rpg`), the UI toolkit and the main menu.

A **game** is a collection: the game's own content mod plus the mechanics it chooses. The project
ships three of them, all running on the same engine mods and switching inside one process:

| Game | Content | Built from |
|---|---|---|
| `cthangover` | `cthangover_game`, `cthangover_murakami` | a visual novel with characters, quests, items, cooking and turn-based battles |
| `rpg_demo` | `rpg_demo_game`, `village_pack` | an RPG Maker style game: tile maps walked with WASD, events on E/F, battles, the map editor |
| `novel_template` | `my_novel_game`, `town_pack` | the smallest playable novel, a starting point for a new story |

| Layer | Location | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| Kernel | `Core/` | Genre-neutral services: mod pipeline, data resolvers, scenes, saves, events, UI hosting |
| Engine mods | `mods/<subject>_base`, mechanics | Subsystems a game may take: shell, dialogues and DSL, characters, battles, tile world, menu |
| Game | `collections/<game>` | The set of mechanics and the entry scene of one game |
| Content mods | `mods/<game>_game`, `mods/<theme>_pack` | Scenes, maps, scenarios, characters, art and text of a game, or reusable art |

The kernel never depends on a game or a genre. A mod that needs another one declares it in `depends` — see
Configs — manifest.json.

===== DOC docs/mods/avatars/index.md: Avatars =====
Character portrait images displayed during dialogue scenes, positioned via `first=` (left, player) and `second=` (right, NPC) parameters.

## Directory structure

```filestree
avatars/
└── {character_name}/
    └── {expression}.png
```

## Real example — Murakami

From `mods/cthangover_murakami/avatars/murakami/`:

```filestree
murakami/
├── normal_1.png      # neutral expression
├── normal_2.png      # neutral variant
├── fear_1.png        # scared
└── fear_2.png        # terrified
```

The compile-time catalog (`Avatars.cs`) additionally lists `murakami/normal_3`. Expressions are referenced as `<character>/<expression>` without the `.png` extension in DSL parameters (`first=` / `second=`).

## Usage in scenarios

Avatars appear in dialogue lines through the `text` command:

```scenario
text "- Help me..." key=murakami/help second=murakami/fear_1
text "- What are you doing here alone?" key=murakami/alone first=marao/think
```

- `first=` — left-side speaker (usually the player)
- `second=` — right-side speaker (usually the NPC)
- The value is `{character}/{expression}` — no `.png` extension

## Adding a new avatar set

1. Create `avatars/{character_name}/` in your mod
2. Add `.png` files for each expression you need
3. The character's JSON definition sets `"Image"` to the base path:

```jsonc
{
  "Id": "Murakami",                                        // Unique character identifier
  "Image": "characters/player/murakami"                    // Base path to character textures
}
```

4. Use in scenarios: `first=murakami/normal_1` or `second=murakami/fear_1`

## Multiple characters in dialogue

The same expression naming convention works for any character:

```filestree
avatars/
├── marao/
│   ├── sad.png
│   ├── smile.png
│   └── think.png
└── murakami/
    ├── normal_1.png
    ├── normal_2.png
    ├── fear_1.png
    └── fear_2.png
```

Note that the on-disk set and the compile-time catalog may differ slightly: `Avatars.cs` lists additional marao expressions (`angry`, `what`, `relax`) and a murakami `normal_3`, while marao's `sad` exists only as a file. Always check the mod's avatar directory for what is actually available.

## Format requirements

- **Format:** PNG with transparency
- **Resolution:** 512×512 recommended, but any square power-of-two works
- **Naming:** lowercase, underscores for multi-word expressions (`fear_1`, `very_happy`)
- The extension is omitted in scenario files — the engine resolves `{character}/{expression}` to `avatars/{character}/{expression}.png`

===== DOC docs/mods/backgrounds/index.md: Backgrounds =====
Scene background images with optional depth maps for parallax effects and dynamic lighting.

## Directory structure

```filestree
backgrounds/
└── {scene_name}/
    ├── {image_name}.png
    └── {image_name}_depth.png   # optional depth map
```

## Real example — Murakami meeting

From `mods/cthangover_murakami/backgrounds/town/meetingmurakami/`:

```filestree
meetingmurakami/
├── 00002-1920617752.png       # wolf tracks at the door
├── 00002-1920617752_depth.png
├── 00014-1724512349.png       # Murakami at her door
├── 00014-1724512349_depth.png
├── 00024-3950100873.png
├── 00024-3950100873_depth.png
├── 00031-3535505844.png       # werewolf reveal
├── 00031-3535505844_depth.png
├── 00055-1920617752.png
├── 00055-1920617752_depth.png
├── 00078-2783395178.png       # inside, different angle
├── 00078-2783395178_depth.png
├── 00079-2783395178.png       # inside the house
├── 00079-2783395178_depth.png
├── 00136-3647329618.png       # dark room
└── 00136-3647329618_depth.png
```

Each background has a matching `_depth.png` — a grayscale image where brightness encodes the Z-depth of each pixel. The `light_set` command uses it to cast dynamic shadows.

## Usage in scenarios

```scenario
background "town/meetingmurakami/00002-1920617752"
text "[Wolf tracks lead straight to the doorstep]" key=meeting/after_wolf/tracks

background "town/meetingmurakami/00014-1724512349"
text "- Dear lady!" key=meeting/after_wolf/greeting first=marao/think
```

The path is relative to `backgrounds/` — no `.png` extension needed.

## Scene default background

Each scene definition can specify a default background that loads when entering the scene:

```jsonc
{
  "name": "home_kitchen",                                  // Scene identifier
  "defaultBackground": "home/kitchen",                     // Background image loaded when entering the scene
  "defaultScenario": "scenarios/home/home_kitchen_default.scenario" // Default scenario to run on scene enter
}
```

Mods override backgrounds by setting a higher `priority` in their `.scenario` file.

## Adding a new background

1. Create `backgrounds/{scene}/{name}.png` in your mod
2. Optionally create a `{name}_depth.png` grayscale depth map
3. Reference in scenario: `background "scene/name"`
4. If you want it as the default, update the scene JSON's `defaultBackground`

## AI-generated backgrounds workflow

The project uses AI-generated (Stable Diffusion / Flux) images for backgrounds. Typical workflow:

1. Generate a scene description prompt
2. Get the base PNG
3. Generate a depth map variant using a depth estimator or ControlNet
4. Place both files in the appropriate folder

===== DOC docs/mods/battle/index.md: Battle System =====
The battle engine is a pluggable, mod-driven system that lives entirely in the `turn_battle_base` mod. Each battle ruleset is implemented as a mod (e.g. `card_battle`, `ff_battle`) following the `IBattleCore` interface; the platform types (`BattleData`, `BattleCoreRegistry`, `BattleSceneContext`, action executors, status effects, victory screen) all ship in `turn_battle_base`, so the core only knows generic mod infrastructure. Battles are initiated from scenario scripts via `action battle.init`.

## Architecture overview

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    subgraph DSL["Scenario DSL"]
        A["action battle.init<br/>scene=<br/>enemies=<br/>quest_id=<br/>new_tag="]
    end
    subgraph Mod1["Mod / turn_battle_base"]
        B["BattleInitAction<br/>ctx.GetParam<br/>BattleService.Init<br/>Log event"]
        C["BattleRequest.Current<br/>BattleData<br/>Quest / NewTag<br/>EnemiesCards<br/>Background / DepthMap / AlbedoMap<br/>BattleSide<br/>ActiveBattleCore<br/>ReturnScene"]
        S["SceneRouteRegistry<br/>'Battle' -> scenes/battle/battle.tscn"]
    end
    subgraph Mod2["Mod (e.g. card_battle)"]
        D["IBattleCore.Init()<br/>Character panels<br/>Action executors<br/>UI widgets<br/>Status effects"]
    end
    A --> B --> C --> D
    S -.->|"switch_scene Battle"| D
```

The scenario switches to the battle view with `switch_scene Battle`; the mod registered that logical name as a scene route with the core, so `SceneManager` routes it to the mod-relative tscn (with fade transition and combat music) without any battle knowledge in the core.

## Key interfaces

### `IBattleCore` — Combat ruleset

Every battle system mod defines a class implementing `IBattleCore` (`mods/turn_battle_base/src/Adapters/IBattleCore.cs`):

| Member | Description |
|---|---|
| `Id` | Unique string ID used by `battle.set_core` |
| `ActionProvider` | Returns `IActionExecutorProvider` for overriding action executors |
| `Init(Character[] playerChars, Character[] enemyChars, IBattleContext ctx)` | Called when battle starts — sets up UI, spawns enemies |
| `Start()` | Called after Init — begins the battle loop |

Cores are discovered by `TurnBattleSceneInitializer`: it rescans mod assemblies as they announce themselves and calls `BattleCoreRegistry.RegisterAssembly`. Mods that add a core should declare `"depends": ["turn_battle_base>=1"]` so their assembly compiles after the platform.

### `IActionExecutor` — Ability resolution

Each battle action type (attack, defence, stun, item, etc.) has an executor:

| Member | Description |
|---|---|
| `ActionId` | Unique action identifier |
| `Execute(action, user, target)` | Resolves the action against target |

Executors are registered through `ActionExecutorHub`, which routes actions by ID at runtime.

GDScript mods can register executors as well — a `.gd` file with `get_action_id()` + `execute(action, user, target)` is auto-registered into the global executor registry; see GDScript in Mods for the dictionary-based contract.

### `IBattleContext` — Engine bridge

Bridge from the battle core back to the Godot scene so cores stay engine-agnostic:

| Member | Description |
|---|---|
| `ApplyDamage(source, target, rawDamage, defence)` | Applies `max(0, rawDamage - defence)` to the target's Health directly (no event — the UI layer polls) |
| `ScheduleAnimation(IBattleAnimation)` | Schedules visual feedback for playback |
| `EndTurn(BattleSide side)` | Phase signal: the given side ends its turn |
| `EndBattle(BattleSide winner)` | Ends the battle with the given winner |
| `RootNode` | Godot `Node` anchor for instantiating nodes (e.g. damage numbers) |

## Battle initialization flow

1. Scenario runs `action battle.set_core core=card_battle` — selects the ruleset by ID. The registry is a case-sensitive dictionary and **throws on an unknown ID**; built-in IDs are `card_battle` and `ff_battle`
2. Scenario runs `action battle.init scene=... enemies=... quest_id=... new_tag=...`
3. `BattleInitAction` reads dialog variables and calls `BattleService.Init()`
4. `BattleData` captures the current background
5. `BattleData.InitBattle` resolves enemy character IDs to `Character` instances via `CharacterFactory`; unknown IDs get a fallback template character (20 HP, 3 action points)
6. `BattleService.Init` stores the encounter in `BattleRequest.Current` (the mod's static hand-off slot — the core runtime data no longer carries battle state)
7. Scenario switches to `battle`, the core routes it through `SceneRouteRegistry`, and `BattleSceneContext` resolves the active `IBattleCore` and calls `Init()` then `Start()`

**Important**: `battle.init` must run *after* the background has been set in the scenario, otherwise the battle gets a null backdrop.

## Status effects

Status effects (buff, debuff, stun) are applied during battle via the `StatusEffectQueue` of each combatant. Queues are not fields of `Character` — battle code obtains them through `StatusEffectService.Of(character)`, a `ConditionalWeakTable` side table owned by the turn-battle mod. Effects have a duration in turns and expire automatically.

See Status Effects for the JSON definition format.

## Victory screen listeners (winground)

The victory screen (`Winground.tscn`, driven by `WingroundBehaviour`) exposes an extension point for mods that want to modify EXP awards, alter generated loot, or run logic right before the UI appears. Implement `IWingroundListener` (`mods/turn_battle_base/src/IWingroundListener.cs`):

| Member | Description |
|---|---|
| `Id` | Unique identifier (convention: `"mod_id.listener_name"`), used in logs and dependency ordering |
| `OnBeforeExpAward(WingroundContext)` | EXP data is populated but not yet applied to characters; the loot list is still empty |
| `OnAfterLootGenerated(WingroundContext)` | Loot has been rolled from defeated enemies but not yet added to inventory — mutate freely |
| `OnBeforeShow(WingroundContext)` | All processing done; final chance to mutate data before the victory UI is shown |

Hooks fire in that order during `WingroundBehaviour.Show`. The same `WingroundContext` instance is passed through every phase, so mutations made by earlier hooks are visible to later hooks and listeners.

Registration goes through the `WingroundListenerRegistry` singleton:

- **C# mods**: implement `IWingroundListener` with a parameterless constructor — `TurnBattleSceneInitializer` scans mod assemblies and registers implementations automatically (replaces the old core-side fan-out; the battle mod no longer lives in the core)
- Exceptions thrown by individual listeners are logged and swallowed so one broken mod cannot break the victory screen for others

### `WingroundContext`

Mutable context shared by all listeners (`mods/turn_battle_base/src/WingroundContext.cs`):

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `PlayerExpGained` | `Dictionary<string, int>` | Per-character EXP earned, keyed by character ID |
| `DefeatedEnemies` | `List<Character>` | Enemies defeated this battle; drives loot generation and recruitment rolls |
| `GeneratedLoot` | `List<ItemContainer>` | Loot items — add, remove, or modify entries before they are committed to inventory |
| `BattleData` | `BattleData` | Reference to the battle descriptor shared with the quest system — do not mutate the object itself |

### GDScript variant

A `.gd` script exposing `get_winground_listener_id()` returning a unique string, plus any of the optional hooks `on_winground_before_exp(ctx)` / `on_winground_after_loot(ctx)` / `on_winground_before_show(ctx)`, is detected by `TurnBattleGDScriptHook`, wrapped in a `GDWingroundListener`, and registered automatically. All three hooks are optional — only the methods the script actually implements are called.

The C# context is marshalled into a plain `Dictionary` before each call and synced back afterwards: mutations to `exp_gained` and `generated_loot` propagate to the C# victory pipeline, while `defeated_enemies` are read-only dictionary snapshots whose changes are *not* written back. Dictionaries replace the old RefCounted DTOs because mod-authored C# classes get no Godot source generation and would be invisible to GDScript.

```gdscript
extends RefCounted

func get_winground_listener_id() -> String:
    return "my_mod.double_exp"

func on_winground_before_exp(ctx) -> void:
    for id in ctx["exp_gained"]:
        ctx["exp_gained"][id] *= 2
```

## Battle speed control

`BattleSpeedControl` (`mods/turn_battle_base/src/BattleSpeedControl.cs`) is the x1/x4/x8 toggle bar shown during battle:

| Member | Description |
|---|---|
| `ApplySavedSpeed()` | Restores `Engine.TimeScale` from `GameData.Settings.BattleSpeed` (out-of-range values fall back to x1); called from the widget constructor |
| `ResetToNormal()` | Resets `Engine.TimeScale` to 1.0 *without* saving — the battle scene calls it when the battle ends so menus run at normal speed |

Picking a speed sets `Engine.TimeScale`, which accelerates the entire game loop (not just animations), and persists the choice to `GameData.Settings.BattleSpeed`.

## Animation layer

Visual feedback is decoupled from combat logic so battle cores stay engine-agnostic (`mods/turn_battle_base/src/Adapters/`):

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| `IBattleAnimation` | Data-only description: `Source`/`Target` characters, `AnimationType` dispatch key, `Speed` multiplier, open-ended `Parameters` dictionary |
| `IBattleAnimationPlayer` | Plays one animation type — `Play(anim, onComplete)` must invoke the callback so the action machine can advance |
| `AnimationRegistry` | Singleton index (`Instance`) keyed by `AnimationType`; `Register()` replaces any existing player for the same key, `Get()` looks up |

Executors never play animations directly — they request them via `IBattleContext.ScheduleAnimation(IBattleAnimation)`, and the playback layer resolves the matching player from the registry.

## Executor routing recap

`ActionExecutorHub` resolves an action ID in two tiers:

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    A["Execute(action, user, target)"] --> B{"Active provider<br/>set?"}
    B -- "yes" --> C["provider.GetExecutor(id)"]
    C -- "miss" --> D["Global registry<br/>RegisterGlobal(executor)"]
    B -- "no" --> D
    D -- "miss" --> E["Result = false"]
    C -- "hit" --> F["executor.Execute()"]
    D -- "hit" --> F
```

- **Global tier**: `ActionExecutorHub.Instance.RegisterGlobal(executor)` — the fallback pool, including auto-registered GDScript executors (a `.gd` file with `get_action_id()` + `execute()`)
- **Provider tier**: the active battle core installs per-core overrides via `SetActiveProvider(IActionExecutorProvider)`
- Lookup order: active provider first, global registry as fallback; no match (or a null action) yields `ChangedAttributes { Result = false }`

## Creating a custom battle system mod

1. Create a new mod with `manifest.json`:
```jsonc
   {
     "name": "MyBattle",                                   // Human-readable mod name
     "description": "Custom battle system",                // Short description
     "sources": ["src/**/*.cs"],                           // Glob patterns for C# source files
     "depends": ["cthangover_game", "turn_battle_base"]  // Battle platform dependency
   }
```

2. Implement `IBattleCore`:
   ```csharp
   public class MyBattleCore : IBattleCore
   {
       public string Id => "MyBattle";
       public IActionExecutorProvider ActionProvider => new MyActionProvider();

       public void Init(Character[] playerChars, Character[] enemyChars, IBattleContext ctx)
       {
           // Create enemy panels, character widgets, action buttons
       }

       public void Start()
       {
           // Begin turn loop
       }
   }
   ```

3. Implement `IActionExecutorProvider` to provide your action executors:
   ```csharp
   public class MyActionProvider : IActionExecutorProvider
   {
       public IActionExecutor GetExecutor(string actionId)
       {
           return actionId switch
           {
               "mybattle.attack" => new MyAttackExecutor(),
               "mybattle.defend" => new MyDefendExecutor(),
               _ => null
           };
       }
   }
   ```

4. In scenarios, select your core before initiating battle:
   ```scenario
   action battle.set_core core=MyBattle
   action battle.init scene=town_entry enemies=wolf_1
   switch_scene Battle
   ```

## Battle-related Godot scenes

All battle scenes live in the `turn_battle_base` mod and are resolved through the runtime tscn pipeline by mod-relative path:

| Path | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `scenes/battle/battle.tscn` | Main battle scene container (`BattleSceneContext`) |
| `scenes/battle/winground.tscn` | Victory screen overlay |
| `scenes/battle/deadground.tscn` | Defeat screen overlay |
| `scenes/battle/exp_report_card.tscn` | Experience report card |
| `scenes/battle/loot_report_item.tscn` | Loot grid item |

===== DOC docs/mods/catalog/index.md: Mod Catalog & Network Repository =====
The mod catalog system lets players browse and install mods from online
repositories. A catalog is a JSON file hosted on a web server; the game
fetches it, merges entries from multiple URLs, and compares them against
locally-installed mods.

## Catalog JSON Schema

```jsonc
{
  "catalog": {                                             // Catalog metadata envelope
    "version": 1,                                          // Schema version of this catalog file
    "updated_at": "2026-10-23T07:10:38Z"                   // ISO-8601 timestamp of last server update
  },
  "mods": [                                                // List of mod entries in this catalog
    {
      "id": "live2d_base",                                 // Canonical mod identifier (matches manifest.json id)
      "name": "Live2D Cubism",                             // Fallback display name (used when no locale matches)
      "author": "ct",                                      // Author credit string
      "version": "1.0.0",                                  // Latest release version (semver)
      "description": "Live2D Cubism C# runtime: model loading, rendering, physics, animation, effects", // Fallback short description
      "locale": [                                          // Localized name and description entries
        {
          "lang": "en",                                    // Language code (ISO 639-1)
          "name": "Live2D Cubism",                         // Localized display name
          "description": "Live2D Cubism C# runtime: model loading, rendering, physics, animation, effects."
        },
        {
          "lang": "ru",
          "name": "Live2D Cubism",
          "description": "C#-рантайм Live2D Cubism: загрузка и отрисовка моделей, физика, анимации и эффекты."
        }
      ],
      "category": "library, visual",                       // Category slugs: story, characters, gameplay, visual, audio, ui, locale, library, engine, overhaul, tools, editor, debug, other
      "tags": ["live2d", "base", "library", "visual"],     // Search/filter tags
      "icon_url": "https://github.com/cthangover/mod_live2d_base/raw/branch/master/mod.jpg", // Full URL to preview icon (PNG or JPEG)
      "depends": ["ui_base>=1"],                           // Versioned dependency strings
      "conflicts_with": [],                                // Mutually exclusive mod IDs
      "downloads": [                                       // Version-specific download entries with mirrors
        {
          "version": "1.0.0",                              // Semver version of this download entry
          "urls": [                                        // Mirror URLs tried in order
            "https://github.com/cthangover/mod_live2d_base/releases/download/v1.0.0/mod_live2d_base-v1.0.0.zip"
          ],
          "size_bytes": 48031383,                          // File size of the zip for progress display
          "archive_sha256": "9d20f4eda7a3ba7b380fefa5964098dc696b3c8399049614e4cd4a03cc6bb1d6", // SHA256 of the .zip file (integrity check after download)
          "install_sha256": "abece6c284f36c35e1b975025dc7b465009f67ce42870c9ad896c5816bb01df4",  // SHA256 of extracted mod content (modification detection)
          "changelog": null                                // Optional changelog for this specific version
        }
      ],
      "status": "stable",                                  // stable, beta, deprecated, or archived
      "min_game_version": null,                            // Minimum compatible game version (semver)
      "max_game_version": null                             // Maximum compatible game version, or null for no upper bound
    }
  ]
}
```

### Root envelope

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `catalog.version` | int | Schema version of this catalog file |
| `catalog.updated_at` | string | ISO-8601 timestamp of last server update |
| `mods` | array | List of mod entries |

### Per-mod entry

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | string | Canonical mod identifier (must match the mod's `manifest.json` `id`) |
| `name` | string | Fallback display name (used when no `locale` entry matches the player's language) |
| `author` | string | Author credit |
| `version` | string | Semver version of the latest release (e.g. "1.0.0") |
| `description` | string | Fallback short description (used when no `locale` entry matches the player's language) |
| `locale` | object[] | Localized name/description entries for different languages (see below) |
| `category` | string | Category slug or comma-separated combination of slugs (e.g. `"library, gameplay"`): `story`, `characters`, `gameplay`, `visual`, `audio`, `ui`, `locale`, `library`, `engine`, `overhaul`, `tools`, `editor`, `debug`, `other` |
| `tags` | string[] | Search/filter tags |
| `icon_url` | string | Full URL to preview icon (PNG or JPEG) |
| `depends` | string[] | Versioned dependency strings (e.g. `"cthangover_game>=1"`) |
| `conflicts_with` | string[] | Mutually exclusive mod IDs |
| `downloads` | object[] | Version-specific download entries with mirrors |
| `downloads[].version` | string | Semver version of this download entry |
| `downloads[].urls` | string[] | Mirror URLs for the .zip archive (tried in order) |
| `downloads[].size_bytes` | long | File size of the zip (for progress display) |
| `downloads[].archive_sha256` | string | SHA256 of the .zip file itself (integrity check after download) |
| `downloads[].install_sha256` | string | SHA256 of the extracted mod content (modification detection) |
| `downloads[].changelog` | string | Optional changelog describing what changed in this specific version |
| `status` | string | `stable`, `beta`, `deprecated`, or `archived` |
| `min_game_version` | string | Minimum compatible game version (semver) |
| `max_game_version` | string | Maximum game version, or null |
| `source_urls` | string[] | *Written by the game, not by catalog authors*: filled in during merge with every catalog URL that published the entry, and persisted in `user://catalog_cache.json` |
| `variant_conflict` | bool | *Written by the game*: set when two catalogs publish the same id/version/title with different content hashes — the entries are kept separate instead of merged so the UI can warn about a likely modified build |

### `locale[]` sub-fields

Each entry in the `locale` array has three fields:

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `lang` | string | Language code (ISO 639-1, e.g. `"en"`, `"ru"`) |
| `name` | string | Localized display name |
| `description` | string | Localized short description |

Resolution order: exact language match → fallback to `"en"` → first entry in the array → top-level `name`/`description`.

## Checksums

Two SHA256 hashes are stored per download entry (per version):

**`archive_sha256`** hash of the .zip archive as it sits on the server.
The game verifies this after downloading to detect transfer corruption.

**`install_sha256`** hash of the mod's extracted content, computed by:
1. Enumerating all files in the mod (recursively)
2. Excluding dot-prefixed files/directories (`.godot/`, `.import/`,
   `.gitignore`) and patterns from `catalog_ignore_patterns` in
   `mod_config.json`
3. Sorting files by path (case-insensitive)
4. For each file: `SHA256(path_bytes + content_bytes)`
5. Final: `SHA256(file_hash_1 + file_hash_2 + ...)`

When the game detects that a locally-installed mod's `install_sha256`
differs from the catalog value, it shows a "Modified locally" warning.
Overwriting such a mod from the network would lose the player's changes.

## Configuration

Catalog URLs are configured in `config/mod_config.json`:

```jsonc
{
  "catalog_urls": [                                        // URLs to fetch catalog.json from (merged in order)
    "https://example.com/mods/catalog.json",
    "https://mirror.example.com/mods/catalog.json"
  ],
  "catalog_ignore_patterns": [".*", "bin", "obj"]          // Glob patterns to exclude from install_sha256 computation
}
```

The **code default** for `catalog_ignore_patterns` is `[".*"]` (`ModConfig.CatalogIgnorePatterns`), which excludes all dot-prefixed entries. The `config/mod_config.json` shipped with the game extends this to `[".*", "bin", "obj"]` to also skip build output directories.

Multiple URLs are merged — when the same mod appears in two catalogs,
the entry with the highest `version` wins for metadata (name, description,
category, etc.), while `downloads` arrays from all sources are merged:
same-version entries have their `urls` lists combined (deduplicated).

The game caches fetched catalogs to `user://catalog_cache.json`.
Open the Network tab in the mods menu (Mods > Network) and press
**Refresh** to clear the cache and re-fetch.

Architecture note: the catalog system provides the *data source* for an HTTP catalog scanner. For details on how a custom scanner turns catalog JSON into `DiscoveredModEntry` items that the kernel can load, see Resources — Scanners.

## Exporting a mod to the catalog

From the mods menu, select a locally-installed mod and click
**Export to Catalog**. This opens a dialog where you fill in the
download URL, category, tags, and changelog, then generates:

- A `.zip` archive of the mod (if it was a folder)
- A `.json` catalog entry file alongside the archive

Categories are pre-checked from the mod's `manifest.json` `category`
field (falling back to `other` when absent); several categories can be
toggled at once.

Both files go into the mods root directory. The JSON snippet can be
copied to clipboard and pasted into the catalog JSON you host online.

Ignore patterns for the SHA256 computation can be customized in
`mod_config.json` via `catalog_ignore_patterns`. The code default `[".*"]`
excludes all dot-prefixed entries; the shipped `config/mod_config.json`
adds `"bin"` and `"obj"`.

## Mod status display

In the Network tab, each remote mod shows one of four statuses:

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Installed** | Same version, same content hash |
| **Update available** | Newer version in catalog |
| **Not installed** | No local copy found |
| **Modified locally** | Same version, but `install_sha256` differs player edited the mod |

===== DOC docs/mods/characters/index.md: Characters =====
JSON-defined characters — both player-controlled and enemies — with attributes, actions, skills, and loot tables.

## File format

Every character file is a self-describing envelope. The top-level `Kind` is the
routing discriminator consumed by `EntityResolver`; `Items` carries the
definitions — a file may define one character or many:

```jsonc
{
  // Entity kind — the type discriminator, not a directory convention
  "Kind": "character",
  "Items": [
    { "Id": "wolf_1", "...": "..." }
  ]
}
```

Directories no longer imply a type: a character file is found wherever it lives
in the mod. Player characters and enemies are the same `character` kind —
`CharacterFactory` resolves both through one collection.

## JSON structure

```jsonc
{
  // Unique character identifier — used in scenario conditions and battle init
  "Id":          "unique_id",
  // Locale key resolved from .properties files for the display name
  "Name":        "locale_key",
  // Path to the portrait sprite inside the mod's characters/ group
  // (extension optional)
  "Image":       "path/in/characters/group",
  // AI behaviour strategy: "base" for random actions, "boss" or any custom AI type
  "Behaviour":   "base",

  // Character level (determines scaling)
  "Level":       1,
  // Action points available per turn
  "Point":       1,
  // Maximum hit points (coerced to minimum 1)
  "Health":      2000,
  // Base defence stat (reduces incoming damage)
  "Defence":     0,
  // Base attack stat (physical damage)
  "Attack":      500,
  // Experience points rewarded on defeat
  "Exp":         5,
  // % chance to recruit after defeating this character (0–100)
  "RecruitmentChance": 0,

  // Action IDs available to this character; supports $add, $remove, $set in patches
  "Actions":     ["physics/attack", "physics/defence"],
  // Loot table — items dropped on defeat; patches match by ItemId for per-field merge
  "Loot": [
    {
      // ID of the item to drop (references an item definition)
      "ItemId":      "food/wolf_meat",
      // Minimum number of this item to drop
      "MinCount":    1,
      // Maximum number of this item to drop
      "MaxCount":    1,
      // Drop probability (0–100)
      "Probability": 100
    }
  ],

  // Any other keys — e.g. custom stats — are captured as extension data
  // (see "Extra JSON keys" below). They do NOT become typed attributes.
  "MyMod/Luck":  7
}
```

## Field reference

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Id` | string | Unique character ID used in scenario conditions and battle init |
| `Name` | string | Locale key for display name (e.g. `characters/enemies/wolf`) |
| `Image` | string | Path to the portrait sprite **inside the mod's `characters` texture group** — extension optional |
| `Behaviour` | string | AI behaviour class: `"base"` for random actions |
| `Level` | int | Character level |
| `Point` | int | Action points per turn (coerced to minimum 1) |
| `Health` | int | Maximum HP (coerced to minimum 1) |
| `Defence` | int | Base defence stat |
| `Attack` | int | Base attack stat |
| `Exp` | int | XP reward on defeat |
| `RecruitmentChance` | int | % chance to recruit after defeat (0–100) |
| `Actions` | array of string | Action IDs (e.g. `["physics/attack", "physics/defence"]`). Supports `$add`, `$remove`, `$set` in patches. Unknown IDs are silently skipped |
| `Loot` | array of objects | Item drops. Identity key: **`ItemId`** — patches match by this field for per-field merge |
| *(any other key)* | any | Captured as extension data into the character's `Properties` bag — see below |

### Image resolution

`CharacterFactory.LoadImage` resolves the `Image` value via
`TextureUtils.LoadFromModGroup("characters", imagePath)` — the sprite must live
inside the mod's **`characters/`** group (e.g. `characters/enemies/wolf_1.png`
for `"Image": "enemies/wolf_1"`). The leading `characters/` prefix may be
included in the value; it is stripped during normalization, and the file
extension can be omitted.

The separate `avatars/` group belongs to `AvatarFactory` (dialog portraits) and
is **not** consulted for this field — see Avatars for that system.

### Extra JSON keys

Only `Health`, `Defence`, `Attack`, and `Point` are typed battle attributes
(wrapped in `Attribute` — see Attribute model). Every other
JSON key that does not match a DTO field (`Strength`, `Magic`, `Fullness`,
`Depravity`, `Discipline`, or anything custom) lands in
`CharacterInfo.ExtraProperties` via `[JsonExtensionData]` and is imported into
the character's `CharacterAttributes.Properties` string-keyed property bag
(`PropertyData.ImportJsonProperties`).

Be honest with yourself about what this gives you:

- **Numbers** import as int (or float when fractional), **booleans** as bool,
  **strings** as strings; arrays and objects are stored as their raw JSON text.
- **Core code never reads `Properties`.** Keys such as `Strength` or `Fullness`
  are inert data unless your own mod code consumes them. They are *not* typed
  attributes and take no part in battle math, hunger, or recruiting unless you
  implement that logic yourself (e.g. in a scenario action or behaviour class).

## Real examples

### Player character — `cthangover_game/characters/player/marao.json`

```jsonc
{
  // Player character ID — referenced throughout the game
  "Id": "Marao",
  // Locale key for the character's display name
  "Name": "characters/player/marao",
  // Portrait sprite under characters/player/marao.png
  "Image": "characters/player/marao",
  // Starting level
  "Level": 1,
  // Action points per turn
  "Point": 1,
  // Base health pool
  "Health": 16,
  // Base defence
  "Defence": 0,
  // Base attack damage
  "Attack": 5,
  // Available combat actions
  "Actions": ["physics/attack", "physics/defence"]
}
```

### Enemy — `cthangover_game/characters/enemies/wolf_1.json`

```jsonc
{
  // Unique enemy ID for this wolf variant
  "Id": "wolf_1",
  // Locale key for the display name
  "Name": "characters/enemies/wolf",
  // Portrait sprite under characters/enemies/wolf_1.png
  "Image": "characters/enemies/wolf_1",
  // AI behaviour: "base" picks random actions
  "Behaviour": "base",
  // Enemy level
  "Level": 1,
  // Action points per turn
  "Point": 1,
  // Hit points (low — this is a weak enemy)
  "Health": 15,
  // Base defence
  "Defence": 0,
  // Base attack damage
  "Attack": 4,
  // XP granted on defeat
  "Exp": 5,
  // Cannot be recruited
  "RecruitmentChance": 0,
  // Combat actions
  "Actions": ["physics/attack", "physics/defence"],
  // Loot table — drops wolf meat and branches
  "Loot": [
    { "ItemId": "food/wolf_meat", "MinCount": 1, "MaxCount": 1, "Probability": 100 },
    { "ItemId": "resource/branches", "MinCount": 1, "MaxCount": 1, "Probability": 100 }
  ]
}
```

### Boss enemy — `cthangover_game/characters/enemies/werewolf_girl_1.json`

```jsonc
{
  // Unique boss ID
  "Id": "werewolf_girl_1",
  // Locale key for display name
  "Name": "characters/enemies/werewolf_girl",
  // Boss level
  "Level": 1,
  // Hit points (higher than regular enemies)
  "Health": 25,
  // Attack damage (stronger than regular enemies)
  "Attack": 8,
  // XP reward (significantly higher than regular enemies)
  "Exp": 50,
  // 50% chance to recruit after defeat — see [Recruiting](../recruiting/)
  "RecruitmentChance": 50,
  // Combat actions
  "Actions": ["physics/attack", "physics/defence"],
  // Loot table — drops 2 wolf meat at 100% probability
  "Loot": [
    { "ItemId": "food/wolf_meat", "MinCount": 2, "MaxCount": 2, "Probability": 100 }
  ]
}
```

## Battle actions (ActionCharacter)

Every entry in a character's `Actions` array references an **ActionCharacter**
definition (`Core/Characters/ActionCharacter.cs`). Actions define what a
character can do in battle — attack, defend, stun, heal — together with their
targeting category and free-form parameters.

Actions are entities in their own right, collected by `ActionCharacterFactory`
through the shared entity resolver: the file declares `"Kind": "action"` and
can live anywhere in the mod:

```jsonc
{
  // Action files are routed by their own kind, independent of characters
  "Kind": "action",
  "Items": [{
    // Unique action ID — referenced by characters' "Actions" arrays
    "Id": "mymod/fireball",
    // Locale key for the name shown in the battle action menu
    "Name": "characters/action/fireball/name",
    // Locale key for the tooltip text
    "Description": "characters/action/fireball/description",
    // Targeting category: "ToSelf", "ToAlias" (allies), or "ToEnemy";
    // "Passive" and "Active" are non-targeting kinds (see below)
    "Type": "ToEnemy",
    // Icon sprite path, resolved in the characters texture group
    "Image": "characters/actions/fireball",
    // Flat list of "Key=Value" strings parsed into the Properties bag;
    // everything before the first '=' is the key, the rest is the value
    "Values": [
      "RequiredPoint=2",
      "Attack=3.5",
      "AttackType=Fire"
    ]
  }]
}
```

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| `Id` | Unique action ID, used as the factory lookup key |
| `Name` | Locale key for the display name in the battle action menu |
| `Description` | Locale key for the tooltip |
| `Type` | `ActionCharacterType` — `ToSelf` (caster), `ToAlias` (allies), `ToEnemy` (opponents), plus the non-targeting `Passive` and `Active` kinds (see below) |
| `Image` | Icon sprite resolved via `TextureUtils.LoadFromModGroup("characters", ...)` |
| `Values` | List of `"Key=Value"` strings populating the `Properties` bag |

Besides the three in-battle targeting categories, `ActionCharacterType` declares
two kinds handled by the character panel instead of battle executors:

- **`Passive`** — never appears in an action menu. C# code checks flags via
  `CharacterPassiveExtension.HasPassiveEffect(character, key)`, which is true when
  any equipped passive sets that key to `true` in its `Values` (e.g.
  `"IgnoreArmor=true"`; key names are mod-chosen — there is no registry).
- **`Active`** — a one-shot ability used from the character panel outside battle.
  Cooldown state lives in the action's own `Properties` bag: `ActiveCooldown`
  (seconds, declared in your `Values`) and `LastUsed` (timestamp recorded by
  `MarkUsed`). The helpers `IsReadyToUse(action, now)` and
  `GetRemainingCooldown(action, now)` in `ActionCharacterActiveExtension`
  implement the cooldown check.

There are no reserved parameter keys at the definition level — core executors
read whatever keys they care about (the stock physics actions use
`RequiredPoint`, `Attack`, `AttackType`, `Defence`, `Turn`), and custom C#
executors (`IActionExecutor`) can read their own via the `GetStr` / `GetInt` /
`GetFloat` / `GetBool` helpers on `ActionCharacter`.

The cthangover_game mod keeps its actions in `characters/actions/*.json`,
each file a standalone `Kind: "action"` envelope — there is no index file:

```jsonc
{
  "Kind": "action",
  "Items": [ { "Id": "physics/attack", "...": "..." } ]
}
```

`ActionCharacterFactory.Get(id)` returns a **copy** of the cached definition on
every call — action cards carry mutable battle state (cooldowns, modifiers), so
character instances never share one instance.

## Attribute model

The stat block (`CharacterAttributes`, `Core/Characters/CharacterAttributes.cs`)
has exactly four attributes, each wrapped in an `Attribute` object:

| Attribute | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Health` | Hit points (`Value` = current, `BaseValue` = max) |
| `Defence` | Reduces incoming damage |
| `Attack` | Base damage dealt |
| `Point` | Action points consumed by abilities |

`Attribute` (`Core/Characters/Attribute.cs`) mechanics worth knowing when
writing battle code:

- `Value` — current value; setting it fires `OnChange`.
- `BaseValue` — unmodified maximum.
- `Percent` — `Value / BaseValue` ratio for health bars (0 when `BaseValue` is 0).
- `Init(int)` — sets `Value` and `BaseValue` to the same starting value.
- `Copy()` — clones `Value`/`BaseValue`; **`OnChange` subscribers are not copied**.
- `OnChange` — a `ChangeAttribute` delegate (`void(float value, float baseValue)`).
  It fires on **every** `Value` setter call, even when the value didn't change —
  treat it as a notification mechanism, not a diff detector (a fizzle-heal at
  full health still triggers UI feedback).

Battle actions report their outcome as a `ChangedAttributes` struct
(`Source` / `Target` — `CharacterSet` snapshots of both sides after the exchange,
plus a `bool Result` success flag). This is what `IActionExecutor.Execute(action, user, target)`
returns; the executor hub matches executors to actions by `ActionId`.

## Factory behavior

- **Minimum coercion** — `CharacterFactory.CreateCharacter` coerces `Health` and
  `Point` to a minimum of 1 (`Health > 0 ? Health : 1`, same for `Point`);
  `Defence` and `Attack` are taken as-is, including 0.
- **Copies on read** — `CharacterFactory.Get(id)` returns a deep copy per call
  (`Character.Copy()`): `Attributes` are cloned, so battle mutations never leak
  into templates. The clone carries no battle-only state — status effect queues
  are not fields of `Character`; battle code obtains them through
  `StatusEffectService.Of(character)` (see Status Effects).
- **Unknown action IDs** are dropped silently when resolving the `Actions` list.

## Runtime members worth knowing

| Member | Description |
|---|---|
| `Character.Frame` | Small portrait `Texture2D` for list views (party roster, battle HUD). Present on the model but **not populated from JSON** by the factory |
| `Character.Exp` | Current experience points, loaded from the template and carried into party data on recruitment |
| `StatusEffectService.Of(character)` | Active status-effect queue for a combatant. Lives in `mods/turn_battle_base` as a `ConditionalWeakTable` side table — not on `Character` — and dies with its owner; see Status Effects |
| `Character.Copy()` | Deep clone used everywhere the engine hands out characters (see above) |
| `CharacterData.Create(characterType)` | Builds runtime `CharacterInfoData` from the factory template. For an **unknown ID** it does not throw — it returns a fallback entry with default attributes at level 0 |

## Character index files

There are none. Every character file declares its kind itself, so the old
`characters.json` / `enemies.json` index files with `${...}` includes are gone:
adding a character means dropping a `Kind: "character"` file anywhere in the
mod, and later mods override earlier definitions by `Id`.

## Adding a new character from scratch

1. Create a JSON file anywhere in your mod with the `{ "Kind": "character", "Items": [ ... ] }` envelope and all required fields
2. Put the portrait sprite inside your mod's `characters/` **texture** group so the `Image` path resolves (extension optional); dialog portraits, if needed, go separately under `avatars/{id}/` — see Avatars
3. Register locale keys for the name in `locale/en.properties`:
```
characters/{type}/{id} = My Character Name
```
4. Reference in battle init: `action battle.init enemies=my_char_id`

===== DOC docs/mods/collections/index.md: Mod Collections =====
A **collection** is a curated set of mods that together implement a game
mechanic — a card novel, a developer toolset, a battle system with its UI.
Unlike a category label on a single mod, a collection describes a
*composition*: the same mod can be the required backbone of one collection
and an optional layer of another, so mechanics belong to the collection file,
not to the mod manifest.

Collections are the game-construction unit the catalog is organized by: the
player is shown a scheme split into mechanics and blocks instead of a flat mod
list, and can install everything the game needs with one action. The
collection file itself never stores the dependency arrows — they are derived
from the mods' own `depends`, so the scheme cannot go stale when a mod author
changes dependencies.

## Where collections live

A collection is a **package of its own, not a file inside a mod**. A game is an
assembly of mods by many authors — no single mod owns it — so collections ship
next to the mods: anyone can publish a game, a toolset or a block without
touching (or forking) the mods it uses.

A package is a folder or a `.zip` named after the collection id:

```
collections/my_novel/
  collection.json        ← the document (schema below)
  cover.png              ← "cover": "cover.png" — shown in the game picker
  locale/en.properties   ← texts of the collection ("@key" markers), optional
  locale/ru.properties
```

| Source | Location | Precedence |
|---|---|---|
| Shipped with the game | `collections/<id>/` in the game directory (`res://collections/`) | overrides catalog |
| The player's | `user://collections/<id>/` — edited, duplicated and downloaded collections | overrides everything |
| Published in a catalog | `"collections"` array of a catalog JSON (document only: no cover, no locale files) | lowest |

`ModCollectionStore.LoadAll()` merges the three channels by collection `id`
(case-insensitive): catalog entries are inserted first, shipped packages
replace them, and the player's packages replace both. Packages are read through
`IModFileProvider`, so folders and zips work identically. The package's
`locale/` files are merged into the game's translations at boot, before any
mod, so the game picker shows localized names before the game's mods are
loaded. **Mods do not ship collections**: a `collections/` folder inside a mod
is not read.

## Collection JSON schema

```jsonc
{
  "id": "my_novel",                                        // Required. Canonical id, unique across all sources
  "kind": "Game",                                          // Game | Component | Toolset — see "Collection kinds"
  "name": "My Novel",                                      // Display name (falls back to id; see Localization)
  "author": "ct",                                          // Author credit
  "version": "1.0.0",                                      // Semver; picks the newest of same-id catalog collections
  "description": "A visual novel with card battles.",      // Shown on the gallery card and in the details panel
  "tags": ["novel", "cards"],                              // Free-form search tags
  "provides": ["battle_system"],                           // Capability ids this collection implements
  "requires": ["character_system"],                        // Capability ids needed from sibling blocks
  "includes": [                                            // Child collections this one is assembled from
    {
      "ref": "rpg_2d>=1",                                  // Versioned collection reference (same syntax as depends)
      "role": "systems",                                   // Slot role; two required blocks cannot claim one slot
      "optional": false,                                   // True when the block may be excluded by the player
      "selected": null,                                    // Player's choice for an optional block (null = on)
      "label": "2D Foundation",                            // Per-game name override (falls back to child name)
      "note": "Platformer core with map and inventory."    // Per-game description override
    }
  ],
  "mechanics": [                                           // Ordered mechanics — drives the scheme layout
    {
      "id": "scenes",                                     // Stable id, unique within the collection
      "name": "Scenes and dialogues",                      // Frame title in the scheme
      "description": "Scenes, scenarios, dialogue.",       // Tooltip
      "role": "story",                                     // Slot role the mechanic fills in the scheme editor
      "mods": ["cthangover_game>=1"],                    // Versioned mod references (same syntax as manifest depends)
      "optional": false,                                   // Marks the mechanic as skippable
      "selected": null,                                    // Player's choice for an optional mechanic (null = on)
      "locale": [                                          // Per-mechanic localized strings, same shape as below
        { "lang": "ru", "name": "Сцены и диалоги", "description": "Сцены, сценарии, диалоги." }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "locale": [                                              // Localized name/description of the whole collection
    { "lang": "en", "name": "Card Novel", "description": "A visual novel with card battles." },
    { "lang": "ru", "name": "Карточная новелла", "description": "Визуальная новелла с карточными боями." }
  ]
}
```

### Field reference

| Field | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | Yes | Canonical collection id. Entries without an id are ignored |
| `kind` | No | `Game`, `Component` or `Toolset`. Missing values deserialize to `Game` so documents written before kinds existed keep working |
| `name` | No | Display name; the id is used when absent |
| `author` | No | Author credit |
| `description` | No | Short description shown in the UI |
| `version` | No | Semver. When several catalogs publish the same id, the highest version wins |
| `tags` | No | Free-form search tags (`"systems"`, `"cards"`, `"2d"`, ...) |
| `provides` | No | Capability ids this collection implements. The scheme editor uses them to suggest compatible blocks; resolution itself does not enforce them |
| `requires` | No | Capability ids needed from sibling blocks. The resolver reports a problem when no resolved block provides them |
| `includes` | No | Child collections this one is assembled from — see below |
| `mechanics` | No | Ordered list of mechanics; the order drives the top-to-bottom scheme layout |
| `mechanics[].id` | No | Unique id used to group nodes; empty ids get a stable `mechanic_<index>` fallback |
| `mechanics[].name` | No | Frame header; the id is used when absent |
| `mechanics[].description` | No | Frame tooltip |
| `mechanics[].role` | No | Slot role (`"characters"`, `"story"`, `"battle"`, ...). The scheme editor groups the mechanic's mods under this label and role conflicts are detected against it |
| `mechanics[].mods` | No | Versioned mod strings (see below) |
| `mechanics[].optional` | No | Marks the mechanic as skippable (`false` by default). The install plan still includes it until the player turns it off |
| `mechanics[].selected` | No | Player's choice for an optional mechanic. `null`/omitted means on — old documents behave as before |
| `mechanics[].locale` | No | Localized name/description entries, same shape as the collection-level `locale` |
| `includes[].ref` | Yes | Versioned collection reference, e.g. `"rpg_2d>=1"` |
| `includes[].role` | No | Slot role inside the parent; two *required* includes claiming one role is a problem |
| `includes[].optional` | No | True when the block may be excluded without breaking the parent |
| `includes[].selected` | No | Player's choice; `null`/omitted means the block is on |
| `includes[].label` / `note` | No | Per-game name/description overrides; null falls back to the child collection's own fields |
| `locale` | No | Localized `name`/`description` entries — see Localization |
| `source_urls` | — | Written by the game only: catalog refreshes tag each collection with the URLs that published it. Local files should not set it |

A collection with neither `mechanics` nor `includes` shows an empty scheme.

### Collection kinds

`kind` is metadata — resolution does not depend on it — but it drives the
games gallery: only `Game` collections are offered as playable assemblies,
while `Component` and `Toolset` blocks are building material picked in the
scheme editor.

| Kind | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Game` | Finished composition the player can assemble and play (default) |
| `Component` | Reusable block: a mechanic, a system layer, a content group |
| `Toolset` | Developer toolset (editors, validators, the mod manager) |

### Includes — collections made of collections

`includes[]` is the recursive edge of the collection pyramid: a parent game
declares which blocks compose it, each block can itself include others, and
the same block may be included by several parents with different roles.
`ref` uses the same versioned syntax as `manifest.json` dependencies and is
looked up in the merged collection store (user > built-in > catalog).

`label` and `note` are scheme-editor overlays: the player may rename a block
inside one game without touching the shared collection document, so a
component can be called "2D RPG" in the catalog and "Основа" in a specific
game.

### Versioned mod strings

Entries of `mechanics[].mods` and `includes[].ref` use the same format as
`manifest.json` `depends`: `modId[op][version][?]`, where `op` is one of
`>=`, `<=`, `>`, `<`, `=`, `^` or omitted (any version). Examples:

```jsonc
"mods": [
  "cthangover_game",       // any version
  "turn_battle_base>=1",     // at least 1.x
  "card_battle=1.2.0"        // exactly this version
]
```

The pin is used in two places: a locally installed mod that does not satisfy it
is shown as a **version mismatch**, and when installing from a catalog the
newest download satisfying the pin is chosen. The optional suffix `?` is parsed
but ignored — optionality of a whole mechanic or block is expressed with the
`optional` flag.

## Localization

Collection text is localized with two mechanisms; they can be combined.

### 1. The `locale[]` array

Entries carry `lang`, `name` and `description` — the same shape as catalog
mod entries. Mechanics carry their own `locale[]` with the same fields. The
best match for the player's language is picked as:

```
exact lang  ->  flat name/description fields  ->  "en"  ->  first entry with text  ->  id
```

so a shipped collection can present itself in several languages without
duplicating the document.

### 2. The `@key` marker

Any text field that starts with `@` is a **locale key**, not literal text:

```jsonc
"name": "@collections/my_novel/name"
```

The text after `@` is resolved through `TranslationServer`, which merges the
`locale/*.properties` files of **every loaded mod** (keys use `/` as the
namespace separator). If no translation is registered for the key, the raw
text after `@` is displayed — an untranslated marker degrades to a readable
id-like string instead of an error.

Mechanic names get a conventional key layout:

```
collections/<collection_id>/mechanic/<mechanic_id>/name
collections/<collection_id>/mechanic/<mechanic_id>/description
```

Put the keys into the package's own `locale/{lang}.properties` — the
collection carries its texts with it:

```properties
# en.properties
collections/my_novel/name = My Novel
collections/my_novel/description = A visual novel with an optional card-battle layer.
collections/my_novel/mechanic/scenes/name = Scenes and dialogues
collections/my_novel/mechanic/cards/name = Card Battle

# ru.properties — same keys, Russian values
collections/my_novel/name = Моя новелла
```

Because translations are merged from all loaded mods, a separate language-pack
mod can localize collections it does not own.

### Priority

```
locale[] of the exact lang  >  "@key" / flat field (translated if the key exists, raw otherwise)  >  fallback
```

The final fallback is the collection id (names) or an empty string
(descriptions).

## How the scheme is built

`ModCollectionResolver` turns a collection into a `ModCollectionGraph`:

1. `includes[]` are expanded first, recursively, from the merged collection
   store. Each included block becomes a scheme **group** with the child's mods
   flattened inside it; nested blocks collapse into the nearest ancestor, so
   the editor always draws a two-level pyramid. The root's own mechanics
   become leaf groups after the includes.
2. Every mod named by a mechanic becomes a node in that group; if several
   mechanics list the same mod, the first one owns it.
3. For each node, the mod's `depends` list (from the local registry) adds an
   edge **dependency → dependent**, and unknown dependencies are pulled into
   the scheme as external nodes shown in the *Dependencies* frame — including
   transitive dependencies, recursively.
4. Each node gets a state from the live sources:

| State | Node color | Meaning | Plan action |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Installed` | green | Installed, enabled, satisfies the version pin | none |
| `Disabled` | amber | Installed but listed in `disabled_mods.json` | re-enabled on apply |
| `InCatalog` | blue | Not installed, downloadable from a catalog | installed on apply |
| `VersionMismatch` | amber | Installed, but the version fails the collection's pin | reported as a warning |
| `Missing` | red | Unknown to the registry and every catalog | reported; blocks apply |

Inconsistencies found while expanding the pyramid are reported as **problems**
instead of failing resolution:

| Problem | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Missing block | an `includes[]` reference points at a collection nobody provides |
| Cycle | the include chain references a collection already on the active stack |
| Role conflict | two *required* includes claim the same role slot (optional blocks never conflict) |
| Unmet requirement | a resolved collection `requires` a capability no block `provides` |

The scheme is resolved fresh every time the collections screen opens or a
catalog refresh finishes, so it always reflects the current registry, disabled
store and catalog cache.

## The Mods & Collections hub

The mod manager contributes a single main-menu button, **Mods & Collections**
(with a counter of problems requiring attention), which opens one screen with
two tabs — **Mods** (the default) and **Collections**. The tab choice is
remembered, and the two screens stay cross-linked: a mod card lists the
collections that use it and links straight to their schemes, and scheme
problems link back to the mod catalog search.

### Collections tab — the games gallery

The first level of the Collections tab is a gallery of **games**: collections
with `"kind": "Game"`. Every card shows the cover, author/version/origin, the
mod count, tags and a status:

| Status | Meaning | Primary action |
|---|---|---|
| Ready (full progress bar) | every mod installed, enabled, pins satisfied | **Play** |
| Missing N mods | some mods are neither installed nor downloadable | **Show problems** |
| K problems | scheme errors (missing block, cycle, role conflict, unmet requirement) | **Show problems** |
| Draft | a partially assembled player collection, possibly with unsaved edits | **Continue building** |

Secondary action on every card is **Edit scheme**. Tiles **+ Assemble your own
game** create a new player collection, stored as a package in `user://collections/<id>/`.

How a player assembles a game: pick a game in the gallery → **Install** — the
build plan downloads the missing mods and re-enables disabled ones → the game
shows the familiar "changes take effect after a restart" banner. Mods
installed this way are remembered for the rest of the session
(`sessionInstalled`), so the scheme updates immediately even though the
registry only learns about them after a restart.

Editing the collection of the game that is already running (say, adding the
editors mechanic from the main menu) applies the next time the player enters
that game: **New game** or **Load** sees that the loaded mod set or the
definition no longer matches the collection (`GameCatalog.IsActiveOutdated`)
and switches the mod set in place instead of re-entering the stale one. No
restart of the process is needed.

Applying is blocked while any required mod is `Missing` from the catalog or
while scheme errors are present — enabling a mod whose dependency cannot be
loaded would only make the registry drop it at the next boot. Version
mismatches are shown as warnings but do not block apply.

### Scheme editor

**Edit scheme** opens the second level: a canvas with the game's contours.

- **Canvas** — one draggable/resizable group per mechanic or included block,
  with mod chips inside (state-colored dots); external dependencies render as
  dashed chips. Groups move by their header, mods move inside, the empty
  canvas pans with LMB. A toolbar offers **+ Mechanic** and **+ Block from
  catalog**; a toggle shows/hides dependency links.
- **Inspector** (right side) — tabs: *Inspector* (block metadata,
  provides/requires, actions: add mod, replace block, turn off, remove),
  *Check* (the problem list with "show" navigation to the offending group),
  *Plan* (install/enable counts, missing and mismatched mods).
- **Build bar** (bottom, always visible) — assembly name, **Install N**,
  **Enable N**, **Problems K**, a hint message, a shortcut to the mod manager
  and the primary **Apply collection** button (disabled while errors are
  present).
- **Header** — Save (writes the package `user://collections/<id>/`), Reset and a raw
  **JSON** view, plus the unsaved-edits marker.

## Ship your own collection

1. Create a folder named after your collection id: `collections/<id>/` for a
   game shipped with the build, or `user://collections/<id>/` (zip it to share).
2. Put the document into `collection.json`; the `id` inside must match the
   folder name and be unique across all sources.
3. Set `"kind": "Game"` for a playable assembly, `"Component"` for a
   reusable block, `"Toolset"` for developer tools.
4. Declare `mechanics` (or `includes` of other collections) with versioned
   mod references.
5. Localize: add a `locale[]` array and/or `@key` markers, then put the keys
   into the package's `locale/{lang}.properties`; add a `cover.png`.
6. Ship the package. The store merges it automatically and your
   shipped copy overrides an online catalog entry with the same id; the player
   can override both locally.

A complete example — a game that names its texts through `@key` markers and also
carries `locale[]` for the game picker:

```jsonc
{
  "id": "my_novel",
  "kind": "Game",
  "name": "@collections/my_novel/name",              // resolved from locale/*.properties
  "description": "@collections/my_novel/description",
  "author": "you",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "entry": { "scene": "my_town" },
  "tags": ["novel", "cards"],
  "locale": [
    { "lang": "en", "name": "My Novel", "description": "A visual novel with an optional card-battle layer." },
    { "lang": "ru", "name": "Моя новелла", "description": "Визуальная новелла с необязательным слоем карточных боёв." }
  ],
  "mechanics": [
    {
      "id": "story",
      "name": "@collections/my_novel/mechanic/story/name",
      "description": "@collections/my_novel/mechanic/story/description",
      "role": "story",
      "mods": ["my_novel_story>=1", "shell2d_base>=1", "dialog_base>=1"]
    },
    {
      "id": "cards",
      "name": "@collections/my_novel/mechanic/cards/name",
      "description": "@collections/my_novel/mechanic/cards/description",
      "role": "battle",
      "optional": true,
      "mods": ["turn_battle_base>=1", "card_battle>=1"]
    }
  ]
}
```

## Catalog distribution

A catalog may publish collections in its root envelope:

```jsonc
{
  "catalog": { "version": 1, "updated_at": "2026-09-28T00:00:00Z" },
  "mods": [ /* ... */ ],
  "collections": [
    {
      "id": "my_novel",
      "kind": "Game",
      "name": "My Novel",
      "version": "1.0.0",
      "mechanics": [
        { "id": "story", "name": "Story", "mods": ["my_novel_story>=1"] }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

Collections from all configured catalog URLs are merged by `id` — the highest
`version` wins while `source_urls` accumulates every publisher — and cached
together with the mod entries in `user://catalog_cache.json`. The
`collections` key is optional, so catalogs written before collections existed
keep working.

## Core API

Collections are implemented in the kernel (`Core/Mods/Collections/`), so
launcher code, tools and other mods can use them without the UI:

| Type | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `ModCollection`, `ModCollectionMechanic`, `ModCollectionInclude` | JSON DTOs; `GetLocalizedName(lang)` / `GetLocalizedDescription(lang)` implement the `locale[]` lookup |
| `ModCollectionStore` | `LoadAll()`, `LoadUser()`, `SaveUser()`, `LoadFromProvider(provider)` |
| `ModCollectionResolver.Resolve(collection, collections, localMods, disabledIds, catalogEntries, sessionInstalled = null, language = null, translator = null)` | Builds `ModCollectionGraph` (nodes, edges, groups, problems, states). `translator` (e.g. `CollectionText.Resolve`) resolves `@key` display text; null keeps raw keys |
| `ModCollectionGraph` | `Mechanics`, `Groups` (two-level pyramid), `Problems`, `Nodes`, `Edges`, `InstalledCount`/`TotalCount` |
| `ModCollectionPlan.Build(graph, catalogEntries)` | `Install` items (id, version, source URL), `Enable`, `Missing`, `Mismatches` |
| `ModCollectionNodeState` | `Installed`, `Disabled`, `InCatalog`, `Missing`, `VersionMismatch` |
| `ModCollectionProblem(Kind)` | `MissingCollection`, `Cycle`, `RoleConflict`, `RequirementMissing` |

Example — resolve a collection and inspect its plan:

```csharp
var collections = ModCollectionStore.LoadAll();
var byId = collections.ToDictionary(c => c.Id, StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase);
var collection = collections[0];
var catalog = CatalogService.CachedEntries ?? new List<CatalogExportEntry>();

var graph = ModCollectionResolver.Resolve(
    collection,
    byId,
    ModRegistry.Instance.AllDiscoveredMods,
    DisabledModsStore.Load(),
    catalog,
    language: "ru");

var plan = ModCollectionPlan.Build(graph, catalog);
foreach (var item in plan.Install)
    GameLogger.Log("MY_MOD", $"install {item.ModId} v{item.Version} from {item.SourceUrl}");
```

Writes (downloading, enabling) stay outside the plan: the UI passes
`plan.Install` to `ModDownloader.InstallAsync` and removes `plan.Enable` from
`DisabledModsStore`.

## Start your own game

A game is three layers, and only the first one is yours to write:

1. **Your game's mod** — `<game>_game` (for example `lighthouse_game`): your
   scenes, scenarios and texts. Copy `mods/my_novel_game`, rename the folder,
   `id`, scene and scenario names (prefix them with your game) and locale keys.
   Its scenario lines are written in English (`text "…" key=…`); other
   languages, Russian included, live only in `locale/scenarios_<lang>.properties`.
2. **Packs and mechanics you reuse** — `town_pack` for backgrounds and sound,
   `cooking` or `card_battle` for gameplay. Depend on them instead of copying
   their files: a fix in the pack reaches every game. To change a pack's
   scenario or background for your game only, ship the same name and depend on
   the pack — your version wins (see load order).
3. **The engine** — `shell2d_base`, `dialog_base`, `audio_base`, …

Then copy `collections/novel_template/` to `collections/<your game>/`, set the
`id`, `entry.scene`, the cover and the mod lists, and the game appears in the
picker.

## Real examples

- `collections/novel_template/` — the smallest playable
  game: the game's own content (`my_novel_game`: scenes, scenarios, text),
  reusable assets (`town_pack`: town, home and bar backgrounds, bar ambience —
  the main game uses the same pack) and the engine mods it needs (`shell2d_base`,
  `dialog_base`, `audio_base`). See "Start your own game" below.
- `collections/cthangover/` — the main game, a visual novel: story, the 2D
  engine, characters, quests, items, battles with an optional second battle
  core, Live2D and tools, each as its own mechanic.
- `collections/rpg_demo/` — an RPG Maker style game on the same engine mods:
  its content (`rpg_demo_game`), pixel art (`village_pack`), the tile world
  (`rpg`), the FF battle core with the card core as an option, and the map
  editor (`rpg_editor`) as an optional tool.
- `collections/novel_editor/` — a "game" whose entry scene
  is the editor hub instead of a story scene.
- `collections/dev_tools/` — the developer toolset to add to any game: the
  editors (`editor_tools`), the in-game scenario studio (`scenario_studio`,
  opened by the **Studio** button on the game's toolbar), validators and the
  mod manager. Add it to a game in the collection editor and enter the game
  again — the mod set switches without a restart.

===== DOC docs/mods/configs/game_config.md: Game Config — game_config.json =====
Default game configuration loaded from `config/game_config.json` at startup. Defines audio, display, language, and logging settings.

## File location

```filestree
config/
└── game_config.json
```

## Full schema

```jsonc
{
  // Audio defaults
  "audio": {
    "sounds_enabled": true,       // Sound effects master toggle
    "musics_enabled": true,       // Music master toggle
    "ambient_enabled": true,      // Ambient sounds master toggle
    "sounds_volume": 100,         // SFX volume (0–100)
    "musics_volume": 15,          // Music volume (0–100)
    "ambient_volume": 100         // Ambient volume (0–100)
  },
  // Locale code (UI language)
  "language": "en-en",
  // Display settings
  "display": {
    "window_mode": 3,             // 0=Windowed, 1=Min, 2=Max, 3=Fullscreen, 4=Exclusive
    "resolution_width": 1920,     // Width in pixels
    "resolution_height": 1080,    // Height in pixels
    "current_screen": 0,          // Monitor index (0-based)
    "vsync_enabled": false,       // Vertical sync
    "scale": 1                    // UI window scale factor
  },
  // Logging configuration
  "logging": {
    "enabled": true,              // Master logging on/off
    "console_enabled": true,      // Write logs to console
    "minimum_level": "Debug",     // Min level: Debug, Message, Warning, Error
    "enabled_categories": ["SAVE", "MODS", "..."]
  }
}
```

## `audio` section

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `sounds_enabled` | bool | Master toggle for sound effects |
| `musics_enabled` | bool | Master toggle for music |
| `ambient_enabled` | bool | Master toggle for ambient sounds |
| `sounds_volume` | int | Sound effects volume (0–100) |
| `musics_volume` | int | Music volume (0–100) |
| `ambient_volume` | int | Ambient volume (0–100) |

## `display` section

| Field | Type | Values | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `window_mode` | int | 0=Windowed, 1=Min, 2=Max, 3=Fullscreen, 4=Exclusive | Window display mode (Godot `DisplayServer.WindowMode`) |
| `resolution_width` | int | pixels | Target screen width |
| `resolution_height` | int | pixels | Target screen height |
| `current_screen` | int | 0-indexed | Monitor/display index |
| `vsync_enabled` | bool | true/false | Vertical sync |
| `scale` | int | multiplier | UI/window scaling factor |

## `language` section

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `language` | string | Locale code (e.g. `"en-en"`, `"ru-ru"`) |

## `logging` section

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `enabled` | bool | Master logging toggle |
| `console_enabled` | bool | Write logs to console output |
| `minimum_level` | string | Minimum log level: `"Debug"`, `"Message"`, `"Warning"`, `"Error"` |
| `enabled_categories` | string[] | Active log channels (see below) |

### Available log categories

Categories are plain strings passed to `GameLogger.Log(category, ...)` — **no `LOG_` prefix**.

| Category | Covers |
|---|---|
| `AUDIO` | Audio playback |
| `BATTLE` | Battle engine events |
| `BATTLE_UI` | Battle interface events |
| `CATALOG` | Remote mod catalog refresh/cache |
| `CATALOG_EXPORT` | Catalog export dialog |
| `CACHE` | Mod cache (shader extraction) |
| `COND` | Condition evaluation |
| `CONFIG` | Configuration loading |
| `CONVERTER` | Scenario converter |
| `DOWNLOADER` | Mod downloads |
| `DUPLICATE` | Duplicate singleton detection warnings |
| `EVENT_BUS` | Mod event bus subscriptions |
| `FACTORY` | Factory loading |
| `GAME` | Game lifecycle |
| `ICON_CACHE` | Catalog icon downloads/cache |
| `GDS_MOD` | GDScript mod loading |
| `INTERACTIVE` | Interactive objects/layers |
| `LANG` | Localization loading |
| `MOD_COLLECTIONS` | Collection store/resolver/plan |
| `MOD_HUB` | Mods & Collections hub screens |
| `MOD_MANAGER` | Mod manager screen actions |
| `MODS` | General mod loading |
| `MODS_ASSEMBLY` | Mod assembly loading |
| `MODS_COMPILE` | Roslyn C# compilation |
| `MODS_EXPORT` | Mod export |
| `MODS_INIT` | Mod initialization |
| `MODS_NATIVE` | Native library handling in mods |
| `MODS_DISCOVERY` | Scanner/loader registry init |
| `MODS_REGISTRY` | Mod registry operations |
| `MODS_SAVE` | Mod save dispatch |
| `MODS_SETTINGS` | Mod settings persistence |
| `MODS_ZIP` | Zip provider file access |
| `QUEST` | Quest system |
| `SAVE` | Save/load operations |
| `SCENE` | Scene transitions |
| `SCENE_CTX` | Scene context nodes |
| `SHADER` | Shader operations |
| `TEST` | Test runner (SceneSwitcher) |
| `TSCN` | `.tscn` scene resolution/parsing |
| `TSCN_TEST` | `.tscn` test driver |
| `UNINSTALLER` | Mod uninstall/cleanup |
| `UI` | UI events |

> **Warning**: the `enabled_categories` whitelist filter is currently **commented out** inside `GameLogger.Log` — category filtering is inert, and all categories are logged regardless of this setting. Note also that the `config/game_config.json` shipped with the game still lists legacy `LOG_`-prefixed strings (e.g. `"LOG_MODS"`); since categories are plain unprefixed strings, those entries never match a real category.

### Log output files

Logs are written to `user://logs/cthangover.log`; entries with level `Error` are also duplicated to `user://logs/cthangover_errors.log`. A `--log-file=<path>` command-line argument overrides the log file location.

## Runtime settings persistence

User-adjusted settings are persisted to `user://settings.json` as indented JSON (`SettingsData.ConfigPath`), separate from this config file. The `game_config.json` only provides defaults — changes made in the settings menu are saved per-user. Note that this is JSON, not an INI `.cfg` file.

===== DOC docs/mods/configs/index.md: Configs — manifest.json =====
Every mod needs a manifest and optionally a properties file. These define the mod's identity, dependencies, C# source compilation rules, and scene lifecycle hooks.

## `manifest.json` — full schema

```jsonc
{
  // Canonical mod identifier (falls back to folder name if omitted)
  "id": "my_mod",
  // Human-readable name shown in the mod menu (required)
  "name": "MyMod",
  // Semver version — used for saves and deduplication
  "version": "1.0.0",
  // Author identifier (arbitrary string)
  "author": "author_name",
  // Short description for the mod menu and catalog
  "description": "What this mod does",
  // Glob patterns for C# files to compile via Roslyn
  "sources": ["src/*.cs", "src/**/*.cs"],
  // Glob patterns for GDScript files (.gd)
  "gd_sources": ["scripts/*.gd"],
  // Catalog category slug or comma-separated combination (e.g. "library, gameplay")
  "category": "library",
  // Glob patterns for native shared libraries (.dll / .so / .dylib)
  "depends_native": ["bin/*.dll"],
  // List of mod IDs that must be loaded before this mod (versioned strings allowed)
  "depends": ["cthangover_game", "ui_base"],
  // Mutually exclusive mod IDs — informational metadata, never enforced (see load-order)
  "conflicts_with": [],
  // Declarative GUI entries anchored to shell slots (panel / overlay)
  "ui": [
    { "id": "my_panel", "kind": "panel", "slot": "ModBeforeUIPanel", "scene_path": "ui/my_panel.tscn" }
  ],
  // Title-screen buttons opening fullscreen mod scenes
  "main_menu": [
    { "id": "my_screen", "scene_path": "menu/my_screen.tscn", "button": { "locale": "mymod/menu_button" } }
  ]
}
```

### Field reference

| Field | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | No | Canonical mod identifier (falls back to folder/zip name) |
| `name` | Yes | Human-readable mod name |
| `version` | No | Semver version string (e.g. "1.0.0"). Used by the save system and for deduplication |
| `author` | No | Author identifier |
| `description` | No | Short description |
| `sources` | No | Glob patterns for C# files to compile via Roslyn |
| `gd_sources` | No | Glob patterns for `.gd` GDScript files to compile via Godot's engine |
| `depends` | No | List of mod IDs that must be loaded before this mod (versioned strings allowed — see Load Order) |
| `category` | No | Catalog category slug or comma-separated combination (e.g. `"library, gameplay"`). See the catalog page for the full list |
| `depends_native` | No | Glob patterns for native shared libraries (`.dll` / `.so` / `.dylib`). Copied to the mod cache and wired into P/Invoke resolution by `ModNativeLoader` |
| `conflicts_with` | No | Mutually exclusive mod IDs. **Informational only** — parsed and copied into catalog exports, never enforced at runtime (see the load order warning) |
| `ui` | No | Declarative GUI entries anchored to named shell slots — toolbar-toggled `panel`s and code-driven `overlay`s, collected by `UiRegistry` |
| `main_menu` | No | Title-screen buttons; each opens a fullscreen mod `.tscn`, collected by `MenuRegistry` (section `mods`) |

### Sources — glob patterns

- `"src/*.cs"` — all `.cs` files in `src/`, one level deep
- `"src/**/*.cs"` — all `.cs` files recursively

### GDScript sources — `gd_sources`
