| `defaultScenario` | Path to the scenario file that runs when the scene loads (relative to mod root) |

## Real examples

From `mods/cthangover_game/scenes/`:

```jsonc
{
  "name": "home_kitchen",                                 // Scene ID — used with switch_scene and ui[] entries
  "defaultBackground": "home/kitchen",                    // Background image path (relative to backgrounds/)
  "defaultAmbient": "",                                   // No ambient music for this scene
  "defaultScenario": "scenarios/home/home_kitchen_default.scenario"  // Entry-point scenario
}
```

```jsonc
{
  "name": "home_vestibule",                                   // Scene ID — vestibule/entrance of the player's home
  "defaultBackground": "home/vestibule",                      // Background image path (relative to backgrounds/)
  "defaultAmbient": "",                                       // No ambient music for this scene
  "defaultScenario": "scenarios/home/home_vestibule_default.scenario"  // Entry-point scenario
}
```

## Scene navigation

Scenes are connected through the `switch_scene` DSL command:

```scenario
switch_scene home_vestibule
end
```

And through `option` jumps that lead to `switch_scene`:

```scenario
select "[Where to?]" key=home_outside/prompt
option "Enter the house" key=home_outside/opt_enter -> :enter
option "To the town" key=home_outside/opt_town -> :town

:enter
switch_scene home_vestibule
end

:town
switch_scene town_entry
end
```

## Scene lifecycle

When the player enters a scene, the engine:

1. Collects the scene's events — `.scenario` files targeting the scene plus `[SceneEvent]` C# classes (see Scene Event Metadata) — and sorts them by `priority` with `after:` dependencies resolved
2. Publishes the `scene_entered` lifecycle event on the Event Bus; engine subscribers apply `defaultBackground` (subscriber priority 0), start `defaultAmbient` (5), and update the music type (10)
3. Evaluates every event's `condition:` and enqueues the passing scenarios into the scene's event chain, where they run one after another in enqueue order
4. If no events are registered at all, falls back to the scene's `defaultScenario`
5. When leaving, publishes `scene_exited`

## Hooking into scenes from mods

There is no manifest subscription list; mods extend a scene in one of three ways:

- **A `.scenario` file targeting the scene** — the most common hook. The Cooking mod adds a "Cook" option to the kitchen with a `home_kitchen` scenario at a lower `priority` than the core's default one.
- **A `[SceneEvent]` C# class** — declarative code hook compiled into the mod assembly; runs in the same event chain as scenarios (see Scene Event Metadata).
- **A manifest `ui` entry bound to the scene** — panels and overlays declared in `manifest.json` accept a `"scene": "home_kitchen"` field; the element is then only available in that scene and is hidden when the player leaves it.

## Scenario priority system

Multiple `.scenario` files can target the same `scene:`. The one with the **lowest** `priority` number runs first. If that scenario's `condition:` is unmet, the next-highest priority scenario is tried.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    A["home_kitchen_cooking.scenario<br/>priority: 5"] -->|runs first| S{{"Scene:<br/>home_kitchen"}}
    B["home_kitchen_default.scenario<br/>priority: 10"] -->|fallback| S
```

This allows mods to extend scenes non-destructively by writing additional scenario files with lower priority numbers.

## Adding a new scene

1. Create `scenes/{name}.json` with background, playlist, and default scenario
2. Create the background image(s) in `backgrounds/{name}/`
3. Write the default scenario in `scenarios/{path}.scenario`
4. Add `switch_scene {name}` commands to an existing scene to create a navigation path
5. Register a `[SceneEvent]` class, add extra `.scenario` files targeting the scene, or declare a `ui` entry with `"scene": "{name}"` in your manifest if needed

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/index.md: Scenarios — DSL Reference =====
`.scenario` files are the heart of the game's narrative system. Each file contains a metadata header, a `---` separator, and a script body written in a custom domain-specific language (DSL).

## File structure

```scenario
scene: target_scene_name
priority: 10
condition: QuestId.hasTag("tag_name")
save_allowed: true
is_one_run: false
light_use_time: true
---
# Script body starts after ---
background "path/to/bg"
text "- Hello, traveller." key=locale_key first=marao/normal
select "[What do you do?]" key=choice_key
option "Ask about the town" key=opt_1 -> :town
option "Leave" key=opt_2 -> :exit
:exit
switch_scene home_outside
end
```

## Metadata fields (before `---`)

| Field | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `scene` | string | *(required)* | Scene this scenario targets. Multiple scenario files can target the same scene |
| `priority` | int | `0` | Execution order — **lower runs earlier**. All matching scenarios are enqueued in this order |
| `after` | string | *(none)* | Scenario ID that must come before this one in the chain (ordering dependency) |
| `condition` | expression | *(none)* | Quest-condition expression. Scenario only runs if true. See `dsl/conditions/` |
| `save_allowed` | bool | `false` | Whether the player can save the game from this scene |
| `is_one_run` | bool | `false` | If true, the scenario runs only once per playthrough. After execution, it's skipped forever |
| `light_use_time` | bool | *(unset)* | If `true`, the lighting system uses the in-game time-of-day for light colors/angles; `false` forces static light colors. When the field is omitted the scene's default behavior is kept (it is parsed as a nullable bool) |

## Real examples

### Default kitchen scenario (core)

```scenario
scene: home_kitchen
priority: 10
---
light_set [{"x":0.43,"y":0.58,"radius":500,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"},{"x":0.10,"y":0.27,"radius":500,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"}]
select "A room equipped as a kitchen" key=rooms/kitchen/prompt
option "Leave" key=rooms/kitchen/opt_exit -> :exit

:exit
switch_scene home_vestibule
end
```

### Cooking mod extension (lower priority = runs earlier)

```scenario
scene: home_kitchen
priority: 5
---
light_set [{"x":0.43,"y":0.58,"radius":500,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"}]
select "A room equipped as a kitchen" key=rooms/kitchen/prompt
option "Leave" key=rooms/kitchen/opt_exit -> :exit
option "Cook" key=rooms/kitchen/opt_cook -> :cook

:exit
switch_scene home_vestibule
end

:cook
action toggle_cooking_workbench
end
```

Because `priority: 5 < 10`, the cooking scenario is enqueued **before** the default one. Note that it does **not** replace the default — both scenarios are enqueued and run in priority order. Use `condition:` (or `is_one_run: true`) to exclude scenarios from execution, not priority.

### Bedroom with save_allowed (core)

```scenario
scene: home_bedroom
save_allowed: true
priority: 10
---
select "Your bedroom. You can rest here." key=rooms/bedroom/prompt
option "Leave" key=rooms/bedroom/opt_exit -> :exit

:exit
switch_scene home_vestibule
end
```

### One-run event (interactives test suite)

```scenario
scene: interactives_test
priority: 10
is_one_run: true
---
interactive_add kitchen_lamp
end
```

The interactive lamp is added once, on first visit. Subsequent visits skip this scenario.

## Adding or replacing a scenario

A scenario is identified by its **file name** (without `.scenario`): that is the
id the completion history, `after:` links and the studio use.

- **Add** a scenario to any scene — even one from another mod — by giving it a
  name of your own (prefix it with your mod or game, e.g. `my_novel_town_shop`).
  It runs next to the scene's other scenarios, ordered by `priority`.
- **Replace** another mod's scenario by shipping a file with the **same name**
  and adding that mod to your `depends`. Mods load dependencies first, so your
  file wins; it may even target a different scene. The original mod is not
  changed.
- Two mods that ship the same name without a dependency between them have no
  defined winner — the resource atlas reports this as an error.

## How the engine selects scenarios

1. Player enters `scene: home_kitchen`
2. Engine collects all `.scenario` files targeting `home_kitchen` across all mods
3. Sorts by `priority` (ascending) and resolves `after:` dependencies (topological order)
4. For each scenario in that order, evaluates `condition:` (if present). A scenario **without** a condition always passes.
5. **Every** scenario whose condition passes is enqueued into the scene's event chain — there is no first-match cut-off. Scenarios then execute one after another in enqueue order.

Priority therefore controls *ordering*, not selection: a lower-priority number means the scenario runs earlier in the chain, alongside all other passing scenarios.

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/actions/index.md: Scenario Actions =====
The `action` command bridges the DSL to game systems. Actions can be implemented in **C#** (via `IScenarioAction` + Roslyn compilation) or in **GDScript** (via `get_name()` + `run(ctx)` auto-detection).

## Syntax

```scenario
action namespace.action_name param1=value1 param2=value2 ...
```

Parameters use `key=value` syntax (no spaces around `=`).

## Real examples

### Character actions

```scenario
action party.add id=Murakami
```

Adds the character `Murakami` to the player's party. `type=` matches the character's `Id` field in their JSON definition.

```scenario
action character.send_notification type=Murakami
```

Triggers the "new party member" UI notification for character `Murakami`.

```scenario
action character.add_skill skill_id=slave_master
```

Grants the skill `slave_master` to the player. If the skill defines a `GrantsAction`, the action card is placed into the action pool for assignment in the character panel. Already owned skills are ignored; unknown IDs are logged and skipped.

### Battle initiation

```scenario
action battle.set_core core=card_battle
action battle.init scene=town_entry enemies=wolf_2,werewolf_girl_1 quest_id=MeetingMurakamiQuest new_tag=boss_battle
```

`battle.set_core` selects the combat ruleset before `battle.init` starts the encounter. Core IDs are **case-sensitive** and unknown IDs throw; the shipped cores register as `card_battle` and `ff_battle`. Both actions are declared by the `turn_battle_base` mod.

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| `scene` | Scene to return to after battle |
| `enemies` | Comma-separated list of enemy character IDs |
| `quest_id` | Quest to update on battle outcome |
| `new_tag` | Tag to set on the quest when the battle starts |

### UI panels

```scenario
action ui.toggle_panel name=cooking_workbench
```

`name=` is required. The panel is first looked up as a `Control` node in the scene tree by node name; if nothing matches, the name is treated as a mod UI entry id from the manifest `ui` list and toggled through the UI registry — this is how the cooking mod's workbench overlay opens. A mod can also wrap the call into its own action: the cooking mod registers `toggle_cooking_workbench` for exactly that.

## Available action namespaces

### `character.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `party.add` | `id=CharId` | Add character to player's party (mod `characters_base`) |
| `character.send_notification` | `type=CharId` | Show "new member" notification |
| `character.add_skill` | `skill_id=SkillId` | Grant a skill and its action card |

### `battle.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `battle.init` | `scene`, `enemies`, `quest_id`, `new_tag` | Start a battle |
| `battle.set_core` | `core=card_battle` | Select combat ruleset by its case-sensitive core ID |

### `quest.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `quest.set_status` | `quest_id=Q status=Progress` | Set the quest lifecycle state by `QuestStatus` enum name: `NotStarted`, `Progress`, or `End` |
| `quest.set_data_status` | `quest_id=Q level=3` | Set the quest numeric progress counter |
| `quest.add_tag` | `quest_id=Q tag=T` | Add a tag to a quest |
| `quest.remove_tag` | `quest_id=Q tag=T` | Remove a tag |
| `quest.send_notification` | `quest_id=Q` | Trigger "Quest Updated" UI popup |

> **Note:** The `status` parameter is parsed as a case-insensitive `QuestStatus` **enum name** (`NotStarted` / `Progress` / `End`) via `Enums<QuestStatus>.Parse`. Use enum names in scenarios. Numeric strings such as `status=2` only happen to work because `Enum.Parse` aliases numbers to enum values — do not rely on this. For numeric progress use `quest.set_data_status` with `level=N` instead.

### `scene.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `scene.instantiate` | `path=res://scenes/ui/CookingPanel.tscn name=CookingNode` | Load and add a PackedScene to the scene tree |
| `scene.remove_object` | `name=CookingNode` | Remove a named node from the scene tree |

### `ui.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `ui.toggle_panel` | `name=PanelName` | Toggle a `Control` node by scene name, or a manifest `ui` entry by id |

### `lighting.*`

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `lighting.clear_map` | *(none)* | Reset lighting depth/albedo maps |

### `map.*`

World-map zone control. Every action takes the required `zone_id=ZoneId` parameter; a missing value is logged and the action does nothing. Overrides are stored by the map service and persisted in the save.

| Action | Parameters | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `map.open_zone` | `zone_id=Z` | Open a zone for travel (counterpart to `"locked": true` in the map JSON) |
| `map.close_zone` | `zone_id=Z` | Close a zone — the marker stays but travel is blocked |
| `map.show_zone` | `zone_id=Z` | Reveal a hidden zone on the map |
| `map.hide_zone` | `zone_id=Z` | Remove a zone from the map entirely |

## Adding a custom action

Define an action class in your mod's `src/`:

```csharp
public class OpenTreasureChestAction : IScenarioAction
{
    public string Name => "treasure.open";

    public void Run(IActionContext ctx)
    {
        var item = ctx.GetParam("item");
        var count = ctx.GetParam("count");
        ctx.Log("SCENARIO", $"Gave player {count}x {item}");
    }
}
```

`IActionContext` exposes `GetParam(string)` for reading action parameters plus the game subsystem services and `Log`/`LogWarning` methods (see `Core/Actions/IActionContext.cs`). There is no typed getter such as `GetParamInt` — parse values yourself.

### Registration

You do **not** need to register actions manually: every implementation of `IScenarioAction` in your mod's compiled assembly is discovered automatically by reflection when the assembly is loaded.

If you need to register an assembly manually (outside the standard mod loading flow), call:

```csharp
ScenarioActionFactory.Instance.RegisterAssembly(assembly);
```

If two actions register the same `Name`, the earliest registration wins and later duplicates are silently ignored.

Then use in scenarios:

```scenario
action treasure.open item=food/ration count=5
```

### From GDScript (no Roslyn needed)

Create `scripts/my_actions.gd`:

```gdscript
extends RefCounted

func get_name():
    return "my.quest_activate"

func run(ctx):
    var questId = ctx.GetParam("quest_id")
    ctx.Quests.SetStatus(questId, "Progress")
    ctx.Quests.SendNotification(questId)
    ctx.Log("EVENT", "Quest " + questId + " activated via GDScript")
```

Declare in `manifest.json`:

```jsonc
{
  "name": "My GDScript Mod",                               // Human-readable mod name
  "gd_sources": ["scripts/*.gd"]                           // Glob patterns for .gd files — auto-discovered for actions, conditions, etc.
}
```

The kernel auto-discovers `get_name()` + `run()` and registers the action — no factory or provider code needed. The `ctx` parameter provides access to all game subsystems (Quests, Character, Battle, Inventory, etc.). See GDScript in Mods for the full action context API.

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/dsl/index.md: DSL Command Reference =====
Complete reference to the Scenario DSL commands: 29 keywords registered in the command strategy registry, plus `select`/`option`, which are handled specially by the parser (31 commands total, grouped by category).

---

## Flow Control

### `select`
Opens a choice menu. String is the prompt text (can be a locale key or literal).

```scenario
select "What now?" key=choice/prompt
option "Go home" key=choice/home -> :home
option "Explore" key=choice/explore -> :explore
```

- Must be followed by one or more `option` commands
- The `key=` parameter references a locale key for localization
- If no `key=`, the text itself is displayed
- Supports the same `first=`/`second=` avatar parameters as `text`

A `select` with no following `option` commands is silently dropped from the queue — no error is raised.

### `option`
Defines a choice within a `select` block. Three ways to specify the jump target:

```scenario
# Arrow form
option "Leave" key=rooms/kitchen/opt_exit -> :exit

# Named parameter form
option "Leave" key=rooms/kitchen/opt_exit goto=exit

# Bare positional label form
option "Leave" :exit
```

- Text can be a literal or a `key=` locale reference
- All three target forms are equivalent; the `->` arrow takes priority when several are present
- An `option` outside a `select` block is ignored

### `:label`
Anchor point for jumps. Must start with `:` at the beginning of a line.

```scenario
:enter_room
background "town/meetingmurakami/00079"
text "[Marao stepped inside]" key=enter/room
```

Labels are local to the scenario file. They cannot be jumped to from other scenarios.

### `goto`
Unconditional jump to a label.

```scenario
goto -> :cleanup
```

or equivalently:

```scenario
goto target=cleanup
```

> **Note:** The bare form `goto :cleanup` is **not** supported — it is silently ignored by the parser. Always use the `->` arrow or the `target=` parameter.

Useful for skipping optional content or implementing complex branching without nested options.

### `end`
Terminates the scenario script. Required at the end of every execution path.

```scenario
end
```

---

## Scene & Visuals

### `background`
Sets or changes the background image.

```scenario
background "town/meetingmurakami/00002-1920617752"
```

Path is relative to the mod's `backgrounds/` folder. No `.png` extension.

### `background_color`
Sets a solid color background (overrides image).

```scenario
background_color "#1a1a2e"
```

Accepts an HTML hex color (`#1a1a2e`) or a named color (`black`, `white`, `red`, `green`, `blue`, `yellow`, `gray`/`grey`). Anything else falls back to black. Useful for transitions, flashbacks, or UI overlays.

### `background_show_hide`
Animates the background layer in or out with a fade.

```scenario
background_show_hide hide
background_show_hide show duration=0.5 wait=true
```

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| (positional) | `show` or `hide` (defaults to `show`; any other value is treated as `hide`) |
| `duration=` | Fade duration in seconds (default `1`) |
| `wait=` | `wait=true` pauses the script until the fade animation completes |

### `switch_scene`
Transitions to a different scene.

```scenario
switch_scene home_vestibule
switch_scene Battle
```

The target is a scene name defined in `scenes/{name}.json`.

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| (positional) | Scene name to switch to |
| `speed=` | Transition speed as a float (default `4`) |
| `hidden=` | `hidden=true` transitions showing only the scene background |

The names `mainmenu` and `menu` are built-in: they are routed to the engine's native Godot scenes instead of mod-defined scenes. Any other name may be a scenario scene or a scene route registered by a mod through `SceneRouteRegistry` — for example `battle` is registered by `turn_battle_base` (fade transition plus combat music), so the core does not hardcode it.

### `foreground`
Sets or clears the foreground layer image (rendered above characters).

```scenario
foreground "effects/rain"
foreground ""
```

### `effect`
Plays a visual effect by ID. The syntax is `effect <effect_id>`:

```scenario
effect rain_overlay
```

The ID references a Godot scene (`.tscn`/`.scn`) from the mod's `effects/` resource group, resolved via the effect factory and instantiated above the scene. The script continues immediately — the effect plays independently and persists until its own lifetime ends.

> **Note:** Effect scenes are only loadable from **folder mods** — zip mods skip them with a warning, because `PackedScene` cannot be reconstructed from raw bytes in managed code.

---

## Text & Dialogue

### `text`
Displays a dialogue line with optional avatar portraits.

```scenario
text "- Dear lady!" key=meeting/greeting first=marao/think second=murakami/fear_1
```

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| `key=` | Locale key for the text content |
| `first=` | Left-side avatar: `{character}/{expression}` |
| `second=` | Right-side avatar |
| `hide_color=` | `hide_color=true` shows avatars in shader-based silhouette mode (color suppressed) — useful for flashbacks or unknown speakers |
| `wait=` | `wait=false` advances the script immediately without waiting for a click |
| (inline) | If no `key=`, the text is displayed literally |

### `ptext`
Like `text`, but enables runtime `${var}` variable substitution in the text content.

```scenario
set player_name Marao
ptext "Well met, ${player_name}!"
```

The dialogue box is still shown, and `first=`/`second=` avatar parameters work the same as in `text`. Use `ptext` whenever you need to interpolate variables into a line; plain `text` does not substitute variables. Variables that are not set resolve to `?`.

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| `key=` | Locale key for the text content (substitution still applies after lookup) |
| `first=` | Left-side avatar: `{character}/{expression}` |
| `second=` | Right-side avatar |
| `hide_color=` | `hide_color=true` shows avatars in shader-based silhouette mode |
| `wait=` | `wait=false` advances the script immediately without waiting for a click |
| (inline) | If no `key=`, the text is displayed literally (with `${var}` substitution) |

### `title`
Displays a large centered title, often used for chapter/section headers.

```scenario
title "Chapter 1 — The Hangover"
```

The text can be a literal or a `key=` locale reference; lines without resolvable text are silently skipped.

---

## Audio

### `music`
Switches the background music playlist.

```scenario
music playlists/tavern
music playlists/battle
```

Target is a playlist file in `music/playlists/`.

### `music_play`
Resumes paused music.

```scenario
music_play
```

### `music_pause`
Pauses the current music track.

```scenario
music_pause
```

### `sound`
Plays a one-shot sound effect.

```scenario
sound ui/click
sound battle/hit
```

Path is relative to the mod's `sounds/` folder. No `.ogg` extension.

---

## Lighting

### `light_set`
Configures dynamic 2D light sources. Accepts a JSON array of light objects.

```scenario
light_set [{"x":0.43,"y":0.58,"radius":500,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"}]
```

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| `x`, `y` | Normalized position (0.0–1.0, relative to the viewport) |
| `radius` | Light radius in pixels |
| `influence` | Intensity multiplier (0.0–1.0) |
| `color` | Hex color (`#ffff00`) |

Multiple lights in one array:

```scenario
light_set [
  {"x":0.84,"y":0.04,"radius":600,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"},
  {"x":0.29,"y":0.16,"radius":600,"influence":1,"color":"#ffff00"}
]
```

At most 10 static lights are applied (plus the player's lamp); extra array entries are ignored.

Call with no arguments to reset all lights:

```scenario
light_set
```

### `light_use_time`
Enables/disables time-of-day influence on light color and angle.

```scenario
light_use_time true
light_use_time false
```

When enabled, the shader system adjusts warm/cool tones based on in-game time.

---

### `delay`
Pauses script execution for N seconds.

```scenario
delay 2.5
text "..."
delay 1.0
text "Oh."
```

Extended forms:

```scenario
# Invisible pause — no dialogue box activity
delay 1.5 hidden

# Pause with a caption shown during the wait
delay 3.0 "Meanwhile, across town..."

# Same, but the caption is resolved from a locale key
delay 3.0 "..." key=scene/meanwhile_caption
```

- The duration defaults to `1` second if omitted
- `hidden` can also be given as a named parameter: `delay 1.5 hidden=true`
- `key=` only applies when a text to display was given

### `animation`
Plays a sprite animation. Supports two forms:

Single sprite base with frame controls:

```scenario
animation marao/walk start=0 count=6 speed=8.0 loop=true
```

Multiple explicit sprites:

```scenario
animation sprites=town/rain_1,town/rain_2,town/rain_3 speed=4.0 loop=true
```

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| (positional) | Sprite base path (first form only) |
| `sprites=` | Comma-separated sprite list (alternative to positional base) |
| `start=` | First frame index (default `0`, first form only) |
| `count=` | Number of frames to play (default `1`) |
| `speed=` | Playback speed as a float multiplier (default `1.0`) |
| `loop=` | `true`/`false` — loop the animation (defaults to `true` when omitted) |

There is no `duration=` parameter — control timing via `speed=` and `count=`.

---

## Interactive Objects

### `interactive_add`
Adds a clickable interactive object to the scene.

```scenario
interactive_add kitchen_lamp
```

The name references an interactive definition in `interactives/{name}.json`.

### `interactive_remove`
Removes a specific interactive object.

```scenario
interactive_remove kitchen_lamp
```

### `interactive_clear`
Removes all interactive objects from the current scene.

```scenario
interactive_clear
```

### `interactive_set`
Enables, disables, shows, or hides a specific interactive object.

```scenario
interactive_set kitchen_lamp enabled=false
interactive_set cabinet_door visible=true
```

Supported named parameters: `enabled=true|false`, `visible=true|false`.

---

## Dialog Visibility

### `show_dialog`
Shows the dialogue box UI (if hidden).

```scenario
show_dialog
```

### `hide_dialog`
Hides the dialogue box UI without clearing text.

```scenario
hide_dialog
```

Used for cutscenes where you want the background visible without UI.

---

## Variables & Logic

### `set`
Stores a variable in the dialog runtime's variable store for later use — readable via `${var}` substitution in `ptext` lines and via `ctx.GetParam(...)` in actions.

```scenario
set met_murakami true
set door_locked false
```

The syntax is **space-separated**: first token is the name, second token is the value. Do not write `set name = value` with spaced equals — the parser stores the literal `"="` as the value. A `name=value` token (no spaces around `=`) is treated as a named argument, which is a different mechanism and not how `set` reads its operands.

`set` variables live only while this dialog runs. For state that scenario conditions read, use `flag`.

### `flag`
Sets a **story flag** of the game. Unlike `set`, a flag is game state: it is saved, a new game starts
without it, and scenario `condition:` headers read it. Setting a flag rescans the scene's events, so a
scenario waiting for it can start as soon as this dialog ends.

```scenario
flag door open
flag visits 3
```

```scenario
scene: home_kitchen
condition: door == "open" && visits >= 3
---
text "The door stands open now."
```

Same space-separated syntax as `set`. Numeric values compare as integers in conditions.

### `empty`
A no-op command — it queues a placeholder action that does nothing and is skipped instantly.

```scenario
empty
```

Labels (`:label`) are implemented as named `empty` markers, so a bare `empty` line is also a convenient placeholder slot during development.

---

## Notifications

### `notify`
Queues a notification banner in the top-left corner of the screen. Notifications appear one by one, stack vertically, and auto-dismiss after 10 seconds. Use for quest updates, item pickups, or any non-blocking informational messages.

```scenario
notify "Quest updated: Find the key"
```

```scenario
notify "Received: Health Potion"
```

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| (inline) | The notification text to display |
| `key=` | Locale key for localization (takes priority over inline text) |

Notifications are queued in a buffer — they appear in order and will wait if the screen already has the maximum number of visible notifications (4). The 10-second timer starts only when the notification becomes visible, not when it is queued.

---

## Special Syntax

### `#` — Comments

```scenario
# This line is ignored by the parser
# Use comments to document complex branching
```

### `---` — Metadata Separator

Separates the YAML-like metadata header from the script body. Must appear on its own line.

### `->` — Jump Operator

Used in `option` and `goto` commands to point to `:label` targets.

```scenario
option "Go inside" -> :enter
```

### `${path}` — Include Directive

Inside `.scenario` scripts and other mod text files, `${ path/to/file }` is replaced with the full content of the referenced file at **mod resolution time** — before the script is ever parsed. This happens while the mod loader reads the file (the same mechanism used by `ScenarioResolver` and the other text-based resolvers).

```scenario
# dialogue/intro.scenario
${ events/common_ambience.txt }
background "town/meetingmurakami/00079"
```

- Includes are resolved relative to the mod root and may be nested (an included file can contain includes of its own)
- If the path does not resolve and `path + ".json"` exists, that file is used instead
- Circular includes are detected and reported as errors; resolution fails for the affected file
- Whitespace inside the braces is allowed (`${ path }`)

Do not confuse this with the runtime `${var}` substitution in dialogue text (see `ptext`): includes are expanded once when the mod loads, variables are substituted every time the line is shown.

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/scenarios/dsl/conditions/index.md: Conditions — Quest Expression Language =====
The `condition:` metadata field uses a simple expression language to control which scenarios execute based on quest state and scene flags. Boolean literals `true` and `false` are also valid condition expressions.

## Syntax

```scenario
condition: QuestId.method("arg") [&& || ! expr ...]
```

## Available methods

| Method | Description |
|---|---|
| `hasTag("tag")` | True if the quest has the specified tag |
| `notHasTag("tag")` | True if the quest does NOT have the tag |
| `IsActive` | True when quest Status equals `Progress` |
| `Status <op> N` | Quest status compared against number `N` |
| `DataStatus <op> N` | Quest numeric counter compared against number `N` |

Supported comparison operators (for both quest `Status`/`DataStatus` and flag comparisons): `==`, `!=`, `>`, `>=`, `<`, `<=`.

## Custom GDScript conditions

Mods can add custom condition names via GDScript. A GDScript with `get_condition_name()` + `evaluate(flags, args)` is auto-registered into the condition parser.

`scripts/weather_conditions.gd`:
```gdscript
extends RefCounted

func get_condition_name():
    return "gds.is_rain"

func evaluate(flags, args):
    return flags.get("weather", "") == "rain"
```

Usage in scenario:
```scenario
condition: gds.is_rain
```

With arguments:
```gdscript
func get_condition_name():
    return "gds.flag_equals"

func evaluate(flags, args):
    var expected = args.get("0", "")
    return flags.get("custom_flag", "") == expected
```

```scenario
condition: gds.flag_equals("victory")
```

GDScript conditions take priority over the built-in `QuestParser` — so `gds.is_rain` is resolved as a condition, not as a quest expression. See GDScript in Mods for details.

## Flag conditions

Scene flags are evaluated directly — no `scene.` prefix needed:

```scenario
condition: door_unlocked == "true"
condition: counter >= 3
```

Flags are story flags of the game, set by the DSL `flag` command (`flag door open`) or `ScenarioCondition.SetFlag` from code. They are saved with the game and cleared by a new game. Dialog variables from `set` are a different, dialog-local store and are not visible here.

## Operators

| Operator | Description |
|---|---|
| `==`, `!=`, `>`, `>=`, `<`, `<=` | Comparison operators — valid for quest `Status`/`DataStatus` and flag comparisons |
| `&&` | Logical AND — both sides must be true |
| `\|\|` | Logical OR — at least one side must be true |
| `!` | Logical NOT — negates the following expression |
| `()` | Grouping for precedence |
| `true` / `false` | Boolean literals |

## Real example — Murakami quest chain

```scenario
scene: town_entry
priority: 20
condition: MeetingMurakamiQuest.hasTag("wolf_battle") && MeetingMurakamiQuest.notHasTag("boss_battle")
---
background "town/meetingmurakami/00002-1920617752"
text "[Wolf tracks lead straight to the doorstep]" key=meeting/after_wolf/tracks
# ... scenario continues ...
```

This scenario only fires **after** the player fought the wolf but **before** they fought the boss. It's the middle chapter of a three-part quest.

## More complex examples

### Multiple quest dependencies

```scenario
condition: AwakeningQuest.hasTag("met_marao") && MeetingMurakamiQuest.hasTag("recruited")
```

Both conditions must be met.

### Either/or branching

```scenario
condition: AwakeningQuest.hasTag("found_book") || AwakeningQuest.hasTag("found_map")
```

Runs if the player found either the book OR the map.

### Negation with OR

```scenario
condition: !AwakeningQuest.hasTag("tutorial_done") && (AwakeningQuest.hasTag("woke_up") || AwakeningQuest.hasTag("in_alley"))
```

The tutorial hasn't been done yet, but the player has woken up or is in the alley.

### Status-based progression

```scenario
condition: AwakeningQuest.Status >= 2
```

Runs when AwakeningQuest reaches status 2 or higher.

### Using DataStatus for numeric progress

```scenario
condition: MainQuest.DataStatus >= 10
```

Runs when the quest's numeric progress counter reaches 10 or more.

### Combining quest conditions with flags

```scenario
condition: AwakeningQuest.hasTag("met_marao") && boss_battle == "done"
```

Runs when the prolog tag is set AND the scene flag `boss_battle` equals `"done"`.

## Condition evaluation order

1. Engine collects all scenarios for the current scene
2. Sorts by `priority` (ascending) and resolves `after:` dependencies
3. Evaluates each scenario's `condition:` in that order; a scenario without a condition always passes
4. **All** scenarios whose condition passes are enqueued into the event chain and run one after another — there is no first-match cut-off

This means:
- Conditions filter scenarios out of the chain entirely; a passing scenario never blocks later scenarios from running
- Priority only determines execution order among passing scenarios

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    A["priority: 5<br/>condition: hasTag<br/>secret_found"] -->|"passes"| S["Event Chain"]
    B["priority: 8<br/>condition: hasTag<br/>chapter_2"] -->|"fails"| X["Skipped"]
    C["priority: 10<br/>condition: none"] -->|"always passes"| S
    S --> SCENE["Run scenarios in priority order"]
```

===== DOC docs/mods/scenes/tscn-runtime/index.md: Runtime TSCN Scene Loading =====
Godot `.tscn` scene files can be loaded and instantiated at runtime — after the game is built — through a custom parser and builder. This is the primary mechanism for mods to ship pre-authored Godot scenes that are constructed identically to how Godot itself would build them.

## Why a custom parser?

Godot's native `PackedScene` loading (`ResourceLoader.Load<PackedScene>(path)`) requires the scene to be present as a file on disk. Mods distributed as `.zip` archives cannot use this path. The custom parser reads `.tscn` text through `IModFileProvider` — the same abstract file backend that serves all other mod assets (textures, scenarios, JSON). This means scenes work identically whether the mod is a loose folder or a compressed archive.

## Architecture

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    Lexer["TscnLexer<br/>(tokenizer)"] --> Parser["TscnParser<br/>(orchestrator)"]
    Parser --> Model["ParsedScene<br/>(model)"]
    Model --> Builder["SceneBuilder<br/>(builder)"]
    Builder --> NodeTree["Node tree"]

    Parser --> SecReg["SectionParser<br/>Registry"]
    Builder --> PropReg["PropertyResolver<br/>Registry"]

    SecReg --> GdScene["[gd_scene]<br/>Parser"]
    SecReg --> ExtRes["[ext_resource]<br/>Parser"]
    SecReg --> NodeParser["[node]<br/>Parser"]

    PropReg --> ScriptSkip["script=<br/>skip"]
    PropReg --> ExtResProp["ExtResource<br/>→ PackedScene"]
    PropReg --> SubResProp["SubResource<br/>→ Resource"]
    PropReg --> ThemeOverride["theme_override_*<br/>→ AddTheme*Override"]
    PropReg --> Default["default<br/>→ ToVariant"]
```

Both `SectionParserRegistry` and `PropertyResolverRegistry` use `Reflections.FindAndCreate<T>()` auto-discovery — any assembly can register new section or property handlers without modifying existing code.

## Loading pipeline

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Caller
    participant Lexer as TscnLexer
    participant Parser as TscnParser
    participant SecReg as SectionParserRegistry
    participant Model as ParsedScene
    participant Builder as SceneBuilder
    participant PropReg as PropertyResolverRegistry
    participant NodeTree as Node tree

    Caller->>Lexer: Tokenize(.tscn text)
    Lexer-->>Caller: List of TscnToken (SectionHeader | Property)

    Caller->>Parser: Parse(source)
    loop For each token
        alt SectionHeader
            Parser->>SecReg: Get(sectionName)
            SecReg-->>Parser: ISectionParser
            Parser->>Parser: Open(headerAttrs)
        else Property
            Parser->>Parser: Property(key, rawValue)
        end
    end
    Parser-->>Caller: ParsedScene

    Caller->>Builder: Build(parsedScene, instanceLoader, resourceResolver)
    Builder->>Builder: BuildAll sub-resources
    loop For each ParsedNode
        Builder->>Builder: CreateNode + AddChild
        loop For each property
            Builder->>PropReg: Find(key, value)
            PropReg-->>Builder: IPropertyResolver.Apply()
        end
    end
    alt recursive PackedScene ref
        Builder->>Caller: instanceLoader(path) → recursive parse+Build
    end
    Builder->>NodeTree: Connect signals, call OnSceneBuilt
    Builder-->>Caller: Root Node
```

## TSCN format reference

### `[gd_scene ...]`

```ini
[gd_scene format=3]                    ; uid= and load_steps= ignored
```

### `[ext_resource ...]`

```ini
[ext_resource type="Script" path="scripts/MyScript.cs" id="1_script"]
[ext_resource type="PackedScene" path="scenes/dialog.tscn" id="2_dialog"]
```

| Attribute | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `type` | Yes | `"Script"` and `"PackedScene"` drive node creation and recursive instancing; other types (e.g. `"Texture2D"`) resolve through the ext-resource builder registry |
| `path` | Yes | Mod-relative path. Resolved through mod priority system across all loaded mods. The editor's on-disk form (`res://mods/<mod>/...`) is normalised to the same mod-relative key, so editor-authored scenes work unchanged |
| `id` | Yes | Local identifier within this scene file |
| `uid` | No | Ignored (build-time identifier, inaccessible after build) |

### `[node ...]`

```ini
[node name="Root" type="Control" groups=["my_group"]]
anchor_right = 1.0
script = ExtResource("1_script")

[node name="Child" parent="." instance=ExtResource("2_dialog")]
visible = false

[node name="DeepChild" parent="Root/Panel" type="Label"]
text = "Hello"
theme_override_font_sizes/font_size = 14        ; applied via AddThemeFontSizeOverride
metadata/_tab_index = 0                          ; editor metadata — IGNORED
```

| Attribute | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `name` | Yes | Node name |
| `type` | No | Godot class name (`Control`, `Button`, `Label`, etc.). Not needed when `instance=` is present |
| `parent` | No | `"."` = root node; `"Name"` or `"Name/Child"` = explicit parent. Omitted for root node |
| `instance` | No | `ExtResource("id")` — recursively parses and builds the referenced `.tscn` |
| `script` | No | `ExtResource("id")` — resolves to C# type via mod priority system, creates node via `Activator.CreateInstance(type)` |
| `groups` | No | `["group1", "group2"]` — adds node to Godot groups |
| `node_paths` | No | `PackedStringArray("var1", "var2")` — declares which properties are NodePath editor bindings |
| `unique_id` | No | Ignored (editor-only annotation) |

### `[sub_resource ...]`

```ini
[sub_resource type="StyleBoxFlat" id="style_1"]
bg_color = Color(0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 1)
corner_radius_top_left = 4
```

Built-in builders support: `StyleBoxFlat`, `StyleBoxEmpty`, `ShaderMaterial`, `ImageTexture`, `Gradient`, `GradientTexture1D`, `Curve`, `FontFile` (and `Font` alias).

Custom types are added by implementing `ISubResourceBuilder` — auto-discovered via `Reflections.FindAndCreate<T>()`.

### `[connection ...]`

```ini
[connection signal="pressed" from="MyButton" to="." method="OnPressed" flags=0]
```

| Attribute | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `signal` | Yes | Signal name on the source node |
| `from` | Yes | Source node name (relative to root) |
| `to` | Yes | Target node — **only `"."` (root) connections are applied**. Connections to animation players, visual effects, etc. are skipped |
| `method` | Yes | Method name on the target node |
| `flags` | No | Godot connect flags (default 0) |
| `binds` | No | Not supported |
| `unbinds` | No | Not supported |

## Entry point system

Any root node can declare an entry-point class via a special property:

```ini
[node name="MyScene" type="Control"]
__entry_point = "MyMod.EntryPointClass"
```

The class must implement `ITscnSceneEntryPoint`:

```csharp
public interface ITscnSceneEntryPoint
{
    void OnSceneBuilt(Node sceneRoot);
}
```

The entry point is resolved across all loaded assemblies — mod-compiled code first, then `Type.GetType()`, then all `AppDomain` assemblies as fallback. `OnSceneBuilt` is called after the entire node tree is constructed, properties are applied, and signals are connected.

## PackedScene resolution

When a node has `instance=ExtResource("id")`, the parser:

1. Looks up the `ext_resource` by id to find the `.tscn` path
2. Searches all mods in priority order for the file (same as script resolution)
3. Recursively parses and builds the sub-scene
4. Applies any override properties from the parent node (`visible = false` etc.)

```ini
; Parent scene
[ext_resource type="PackedScene" path="scenes/dialog.tscn" id="2_dialog"]
[node name="Dialog" parent="." instance=ExtResource("2_dialog")]
visible = false                    ; ← overwrites dialog.tscn root visibility
```

When a node property references a PackedScene (not via `instance=` but as an exported field):

```ini
answerBoxScene = ExtResource("2_answerbox")
```

The builder resolves it recursively, packs the result into a `PackedScene` via `PackedScene.Pack()`, and sets the property. This enables scripts with `[Export] PackedScene` fields to work identically to Godot's native loading.

## Mod priority resolution

Both script paths and PackedScene paths are resolved through the mod priority system:

```
.tscn: path="scripts/MyScript.cs"
   → ScriptTypeRegistry: search all mods in load-order priority
   → First mod where the file exists (or the type is registered) wins
```

This is the same mechanism used by `ModRegistry` for asset deduplication. A mod with higher priority can override a scene dependency from another mod.

## Property value types supported

| TSCN value | Parsed output | Godot type (build time) |
|---|---|---|
| `null` | `null` | `Variant` null |
| `true` / `false` | `bool` | `bool` |
| `42` | `int` | `int` |
| `1.0` | `float` | `float` |
| `"text"` | `string` | `string` |
| `NodePath("path")` | `ParsedGodotValue("NodePath", "path")` | `Godot.NodePath` |
| `StringName("name")` | `ParsedGodotValue("StringName", "name")` | `Godot.StringName` |
| `Vector2(1, 2)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Vector2", 1f, 2f)` | `Godot.Vector2` |
| `Vector2i(1, 2)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Vector2i", 1f, 2f)` | `Godot.Vector2I` |
| `Vector3(1, 2, 3)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Vector3", 1f, 2f, 3f)` | `Godot.Vector3` |
| `Vector3i(1, 2, 3)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Vector3i", 1f, 2f, 3f)` | `Godot.Vector3I` |
| `Vector4(1, 2, 3, 4)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Vector4", 1f, 2f, 3f, 4f)` | `Godot.Vector4` |
| `Vector4i(1, 2, 3, 4)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Vector4i", 1f, 2f, 3f, 4f)` | `Godot.Vector4I` |
| `Color(0, 0, 0, 1)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Color", 0f, 0f, 0f, 1f)` | `Godot.Color` |
| `Rect2(0, 0, 100, 200)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Rect2", 0f, 0f, 100f, 200f)` | `Godot.Rect2` |
| `Rect2i(0, 0, 100, 200)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Rect2i", 0f, 0f, 100f, 200f)` | `Godot.Rect2I` |
| `Transform2D(1,0,0,1,0,0)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Transform2D", ...)` | `Godot.Transform2D` |
| `Plane(0, 0, 0, 0)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Plane", 0f, 0f, 0f, 0f)` | `Godot.Plane` |
| `Quaternion(0, 0, 0, 1)` | `ParsedGodotValue("Quaternion", 0f, 0f, 0f, 1f)` | `Godot.Quaternion` |
| `AABB(0, 0, 0, 1, 1, 1)` | `ParsedGodotValue("AABB", 0f, 0f, 0f, 1f, 1f, 1f)` | `Godot.Aabb` |
| `ExtResource("id")` | `TscnPropertyValue(ExtResource)` | Resolved at build time |
| `SubResource("id")` | `TscnPropertyValue(SubResource)` | Resolved at build time |
| `PackedStringArray("a","b")` | `string[]` | `Godot.Collections.Array<string>` |
| `PackedInt32Array(1, 2)` | `int[]` | `Godot.Collections.Array<int>` |
| `PackedInt64Array(1, 2)` | `long[]` | `Godot.Collections.Array<long>` |
| `PackedFloat32Array(1.5, 2.5)` | `float[]` | `Godot.Collections.Array<float>` |
| `PackedFloat64Array(1.5, 2.5)` | `double[]` | `Godot.Collections.Array<double>` |
| `PackedByteArray(0, 255)` | `byte[]` | `Godot.Collections.Array<byte>` |
| `PackedVector2Array(1, 2, 3, 4)` | `ParsedGodotValue[]` | `Godot.Collections.Array<Vector2>` |
| `PackedVector3Array(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6)` | `ParsedGodotValue[]` | `Godot.Collections.Array<Vector3>` |
| `PackedColorArray(1, 0, 0, 1, 0, 1, 0, 1)` | `ParsedGodotValue[]` | `Godot.Collections.Array<Color>` |

Godot native objects are deliberately **not** created during parsing — `TscnPropertyParser` returns lightweight `ParsedGodotValue` wrappers. Conversion to actual `Godot.Variant` happens only in `SubResourceFactory.ToVariant()` at build time, inside the Godot runtime.

## What is intentionally NOT supported

| Feature | Reason |
|---|---|
| `uid="..."` on all sections | Build-time identifiers; mod scenes are not preprocessed by Godot |
| `load_steps=N` | Editor metadata |
| `unique_id=N` on nodes | Editor annotation for scene diffing |
| `[editable ...]` | Editor-only placeholder for inherited scenes |
| `theme_override_*` (nested properties like `theme_override_constants/separation`) | Applied for plain values through `AddTheme*Override`; overrides whose value is an `ExtResource(...)`/`SubResource(...)` reference are not applied |
| `metadata/*` | Editor metadata only |
| `connection` with `to != "."` | Non-script connections (animation callbacks, visual tweens) |
| `binds=` / `unbinds=` on connections | Not used in any scene in this project |
| `owner=` on nodes | Editor concept irrelevant for mod-constructed scenes |
| `format=2` (Godot 3.x scenes) | Project targets Godot 4.x |
| Binary `.scn` format | Only text `.tscn` is supported |

## Adding a scene to a mod

1. Create `scenes/my_scene.tscn` in your mod directory
2. Use standard Godot editor to author the scene (the same file works in both editor and runtime)
3. Set `__entry_point = "MyMod.EntryClass"` on the root node
4. Implement `ITscnSceneEntryPoint` in your mod's C# code:

```csharp
using Cthangover.Core.Scenes;
using Godot;

namespace MyMod
{
    public class EntryClass : Node, ITscnSceneEntryPoint
    {
        public void OnSceneBuilt(Node sceneRoot)
        {
            var button = sceneRoot.GetNodeOrNull<Button>("StartButton");
            button?.Connect("pressed", Callable.From(OnStart));
        }

        private void OnStart() { /* ... */ }
    }
}
```

5. In your mod code, load and build:

```csharp
var sceneNode = TscnScenes.LoadAndBuild("scenes/my_scene.tscn");
GetParent().AddChild(sceneNode);
```

`TscnScenes.LoadAndBuild(string scenePath)` takes a single argument — the mod-relative scene path. There is no mod ID parameter: the resolver locates the owning mod by looking up which loaded mod contains that file, walking mods in load-order priority.

## Extending the parser

### Adding a new section type

Implement `ISectionParser`:

```csharp
public class MyCustomSectionParser : ISectionParser
{
    public string SectionType => "my_custom";

    public void Open(Dictionary<string, string> headerAttrs) { /* parse header */ }
    public void Property(string key, string rawValue) { /* handle properties */ }
    public void Close(ParsedScene scene) { /* commit results */ }
}
```

Auto-discovered. No registration code needed.

### Adding a new property handler

Implement `IPropertyResolver`:

```csharp
public class MyPropResolver : IPropertyResolver
{
    public bool CanResolve(string key, object value)
        => value is ParsedGodotValue pgv && pgv.TypeName == "MyType";

    public void Apply(Node node, string key, object value, PropertyContext ctx)
        => node.Set(key, /* my logic */);
}
```

### Adding a new sub-resource builder

Implement `ISubResourceBuilder`:

```csharp
public class MyResourceBuilder : ISubResourceBuilder
{
    public string ResourceType => "MyResourceType";

    public Resource Build(SubResourceDef def, SubResourceBuildContext ctx)
        => /* create and configure */;
}
```

===== DOC docs/mods/settings/index.md: Mod Settings =====
Mods can expose user-configurable settings that appear in the in-game settings menu under a dedicated "Mod Settings" tab. The UI shows a vertical list of mods (only those that have settings) on the left; clicking a mod name displays its settings controls on the right.

## Architecture

The system mirrors the save system (`IModSaveable`) using a similar dispatch pattern:

```
Mod assembly loaded
  → ModSettingsRegistry.RegisterAssembly(assembly, modId)
    → Finds IModSettings implementation via reflection
    → Stores (modId, instance) pair

Boot time:
  → ModSettingsRegistry.LoadAll()
    → For each registered mod: load user://mod_settings/{modId}.json
    → Call mod.ReadValues(blob)

Settings menu opened:
  → ModSettingsRegistry.GetModsWithSettings()
    → Returns sorted list of mod IDs (only mods with IModSettings)
  → BuildModList() — creates buttons in left panel
  → On click: BuildModSettingsPanel(modId)
    → mod.GetDefinitions() → list of ModSettingDefinition
    → Creates UI controls (CheckButton, SpinBox, OptionButton, etc.)
    → mod.WriteValues(blob) → populates controls with current values

Setting changed:
  → Read all control values into DataBlob
  → ModSettingsRegistry.SaveModSettings(modId, blob)
  → ModSettingsRegistry.ApplyValues(modId, blob)
    → mod.ReadValues(blob) — immediate apply

Reset:
  → ModSettingsRegistry.DeleteModSettings(modId)
  → Rebuild panel with DefaultValues from definitions
```

## Core concepts

### IModSettings interface

Mods implement one class implementing `IModSettings`. It is auto-discovered when the mod assembly loads.

```csharp
public interface IModSettings
{
    IReadOnlyList<ModSettingDefinition> GetDefinitions();
    void WriteValues(DataBlob blob);
    void ReadValues(DataBlob blob);
}
```

- **GetDefinitions()** — Declares which settings the mod has. Called every time the settings panel opens.
- **WriteValues(blob)** — The mod writes its current values into the blob. Called to populate UI controls and to persist.
- **ReadValues(blob)** — The mod reads values from the blob and applies them. Called on boot (from persisted data) and on every UI change.

### ModSettingDefinition

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Key` | `string` | Unique key within this mod (e.g. `"atb_speed"`) |
| `Name` | `string` | Localisation key shown as label (e.g. `"mod_ffbattle/atb_speed"`) |
| `Type` | `SettingType` | Widget type: `Bool`, `Integer`, `Float`, `String`, `Enum`, `Slider` |
| `DefaultValue` | `string` | String representation of the default. Used when no persisted value exists and on Reset |
| `Options` | `Dict<string,string>` | For `Enum` only: maps internal values to localisation keys |
| `Min` / `Max` / `Step`| `float` | For `Slider` only |

### Setting types and UI controls

| Type | Godot control | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Bool` | `CheckButton` | On/off toggle |
| `Integer` | `SpinBox` (step=1) | Whole-number input |
| `Float` | `SpinBox` (step=0.1) | Decimal input |
| `String` | `LineEdit` | Free-form text input |
| `Enum` | `OptionButton` | Dropdown, populated from `Options` dictionary |
| `Slider` | `HSlider` + value label | Range slider with configurable min/max/step |

### Persistence

Each mod's settings are stored as a JSON-serialised `DataBlob` at `user://mod_settings/{modId}.json`. The file contains key-value pairs matching the mod's definitions.

The core handles all file I/O; the mod never touches the filesystem.

### Lifecycle

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Boot                                                         │
│   ModRegistry.Initialize()                                   │
│     → ModAssemblyLoader.RegisterAssembly()                   │
│       → ModSettingsRegistry.RegisterAssembly(asm, modId)     │
│         → Finds IModSettings, instantiates, stores           │
│   SceneManager.Initialize()                                  │
│     → ModInitializerRegistry.NotifyResourcesReady()          │
│     → ModSettingsRegistry.LoadAll()                          │
│       → For each mod: loads JSON, calls ReadValues()         │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              │
┌─────────────────────────────▼────────────────────────────────┐
│ Settings menu opened                                         │
│   BuildModList()                                             │
│     → GetModsWithSettings() → buttons in list                │
│   Select first mod                                           │
│   BuildModSettingsPanel(modId)                               │
│     → GetDefinitions() → controls in right panel             │
│     → WriteValues(blob) → populate controls                  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              │
┌─────────────────────────────▼────────────────────────────────┐
│ User changes a setting                                       │
│   OnModSettingChanged()                                      │
│     → Read all controls → DataBlob                           │
│     → SaveModSettings(modId, blob) → disk                    │
│     → ApplyValues(modId, blob) → mod.ReadValues()            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Implementing IModSettings in a mod

### Minimal example — simple toggles and sliders

```csharp
using System.Collections.Generic;
using Cthangover.Core.Mods;
using Cthangover.Core.Settings;

public class MyModSettings : IModSettings
{
    private float _explosionRadius = 1.5f;
    private bool _debugMode;

    public IReadOnlyList<ModSettingDefinition> GetDefinitions()
    {
        return new List<ModSettingDefinition>
        {
            new()
            {
                Key = "explosion_radius",
                Name = "mymod/explosion_radius",
                Type = SettingType.Slider,
                DefaultValue = "1.5",
                Min = 0.5f,
                Max = 10f,
                Step = 0.5f
            },
            new()
            {
                Key = "debug_mode",
                Name = "mymod/debug_mode",
                Type = SettingType.Bool,
                DefaultValue = "false"
            }
        };
    }

    public void WriteValues(DataBlob blob)
    {
        blob.SetFloat("explosion_radius", _explosionRadius);
        blob.SetBool("debug_mode", _debugMode);
    }

    public void ReadValues(DataBlob blob)
    {
        _explosionRadius = blob.GetFloat("explosion_radius", 1.5f);
        _debugMode = blob.GetBool("debug_mode", false);
    }
}
```

[image: Mod Settings example view]

### Example with enum

```csharp
new ModSettingDefinition
{
    Key = "difficulty",
    Name = "mymod/difficulty",
    Type = SettingType.Enum,
    DefaultValue = "normal",
    Options = new Dictionary<string, string>
    {
        { "easy",   "mymod/diff_easy" },
        { "normal", "mymod/diff_normal" },
        { "hard",   "mymod/diff_hard" }
    }
}
```

[image: Mod Settings example view]

The `Options` dictionary maps internal values (stored in the blob) to localisation keys (shown in the dropdown). The mod provides translations in its `locale/` files.

### Localisation

Every `Name` field and every value in `Options` is a localisation key resolved via `TranslationServer.Translate()`. Add entries to your mod's `locale/` files:

```
# locale/en.properties
mymod/explosion_radius = Explosion Radius
mymod/debug_mode = Debug Mode
mymod/difficulty = Difficulty
mymod/diff_easy = Easy
mymod/diff_normal = Normal
mymod/diff_hard = Hard
```

### Schema changes

**`DataBlob.Version` is NOT persisted for mod settings.** `ModSettingsRegistry.SaveModSettings` writes only the key-value payload (`blob.GetBytes()`), and on load the registry constructs a fresh `DataBlob(payload)`, which resets `Version` to its default of 1. (`Version` *is* persisted through the main save system's `SavePackage` — just not here.)

Detect schema changes by reading keys with defaults instead — missing keys resolve to your fallback values, so added fields need no migration logic:

```csharp
public void WriteValues(DataBlob blob)
{
    blob.SetFloat("explosion_radius", _explosionRadius);
    blob.SetFloat("knockback_force", _knockbackForce); // added later
}

public void ReadValues(DataBlob blob)
{
    _explosionRadius = blob.GetFloat("explosion_radius", 1.5f);
    _knockbackForce = blob.GetFloat("knockback_force", 3.0f); // default for old saves
}
```

## Important rules

- **One `IModSettings` per mod** — create a single implementation in your assembly.
- **Always provide `DefaultValue`** — used when no persisted value exists and on Reset.
- **Use `DataBlob` key-value API** — `GetInt/SetInt`, `GetFloat/SetFloat`, `GetString/SetString`, `GetBool/SetBool`.
- **Always call with defaults** — `blob.GetInt("key", defaultVal)` so missing keys don't break.
- **Key names are persistent** — changing a `Key` creates a new setting; the old value is lost.
- **Provide localisation** — add translations for all `Name` and `Options` values in `locale/`.
- **The `version` field in `manifest.json`** is used by the save system for validation; mod settings have no persisted schema version (`DataBlob.Version` is reset to 1 on load) — handle schema changes via read-with-defaults.
- **Declare `"sources"` in manifest.json** — your `.cs` files must be listed in the `sources` field of `manifest.json` for the compiler to pick them up. See Manifest — sources for details.

===== DOC docs/mods/shaders/index.md: Shaders =====
Godot `.gdshader` and `.gdshaderinclude` files that provide visual effects: pixel-art rendering, scene transitions, sprite-sheet animation, interactive highlighting, and noise generation.

## Directory structure

```filestree
shaders/
├── pixel_art.gdshader
├── pixel_art_post_process.gdshader
├── scene_transition.gdshader
├── timed_sprite.gdshader
├── interactive_highlight.gdshader
├── avatar.gdshader
├── default.gdshader
├── effect_anim.gdshader
└── noise_simplex.gdshaderinclude
```

## Real example shaders

From `mods/ui_base/shaders/`:

| Shader | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `pixel_art.gdshader` | Pixel-perfect rendering at low resolution, preserves sharp edges at any scale |
| `pixel_art_post_process.gdshader` | Full-screen post-process: applies pixel-art look to the entire viewport |
| `scene_transition.gdshader` | Animated transitions between scenes (fade, wipe, dissolve) |
| `timed_sprite.gdshader` | Sprite-sheet animation driven by shader uniforms (time-based frame selection) |
| `interactive_highlight.gdshader` | Glow/highlight effect when hovering over clickable objects |
| `avatar.gdshader` | Character portrait rendering with optional effects (emotion transitions, shadows) |
| `default.gdshader` | Base shader used for regular sprites and backgrounds |
| `effect_anim.gdshader` | Particle and effect animation driven by GPU |
| `noise_simplex.gdshaderinclude` | Simplex noise function — included by other shaders for procedural effects |

## How shaders are referenced

Shaders are referenced by filename (no extension) in the engine. They can be:

1. **Assigned to materials** in Godot scenes via the engine
2. **Overridden by mods** — if a mod provides a shader with the same name, it replaces the default
3. **Applied to specific nodes** via C# code, using the static `Shaders` facade
   (backed by `ShaderResolver`):

```csharp
var shader = Shaders.Resolve("pixel_art");
if (shader != null)
{
    var material = node.Material as ShaderMaterial;
    material.Shader = shader;
}
```

The `Shaders` facade exposes:
- `Collect()` — all `.gdshader` files across mods, keyed by logical name
- `Resolve(name)` — loads the `Shader` resource by name (`null` if not found)
- `TryGetCached(name)` — returns a previously resolved, still-valid instance

At runtime, `ShaderModReplacer.PatchScene(Node)` walks freshly instantiated
scenes and swaps every `ShaderMaterial` whose original resource path matches a
mod-provided shader name. The same pass also performs **texture overrides**:
`TextureRect` and `Sprite2D` nodes have their textures matched by resource
filename against the mod texture registry and replaced the same way — so
`PatchScene` is not limited to shaders.

## Engine-side consumers

The engine ships a dedicated `Control` node that consumes a mod-provided
shader: `PixelArtPostProcess` (`Core/Shaders/PixelArtPostProcess.cs`). On
`_Ready` it builds a full-viewport `ColorRect`, loads the
`pixel_art_post_process` shader via the mod system, and exposes an
`[Export] float PixelSize` property that drives the shader's `pixel_size`
uniform — adjusting it from the Godot inspector updates the effect live.

## Shipped core shaders

Besides the interface shaders listed above, the `cthangover_game` mod ships its own shader
family in `mods/cthangover_game/shaders/`: a set of `2d_cubism_*.gdshader` files
(`mask`, plus the blend modes `mask_mul` / `mask_add` / `mask_mix`, each blend mode
also in an `_inv` inverted variant, and `norm_mul` / `norm_add` / `norm_mix`)
used for Cubism-style sprite masking and normal blending. The folder also contains
`SHADER_CONTRACT.md`, which documents the conventions these shaders follow.

## Minimal custom shader example

`shaders/my_grayscale.gdshader`:

```glsl
shader_type canvas_item;

uniform float intensity : hint_range(0.0, 1.0) = 1.0;

void fragment() {
    vec4 color = texture(TEXTURE, UV);
    float gray = dot(color.rgb, vec3(0.299, 0.587, 0.114));
    COLOR = mix(color, vec4(gray, gray, gray, color.a), intensity);
}
```

## Shader includes

`.gdshaderinclude` files can be shared across shaders:

`shaders/noise_simplex.gdshaderinclude`:
```glsl
float simplex_noise(vec2 p) {
    // ... noise implementation ...
}

float fbm_noise(vec2 p, int octaves, float lacunarity, float gain) {
    float value = 0.0;
    float amplitude = 1.0;
    float frequency = 1.0;
    for (int i = 0; i < octaves; i++) {
        value += amplitude * simplex_noise(p * frequency);
        frequency *= lacunarity;
        amplitude *= gain;
    }
    return value;
}
```

Used in another shader:
```glsl
shader_type canvas_item;
#include "shaders/noise_simplex.gdshaderinclude"

void fragment() {
    float n = fbm_noise(UV * 10.0, 4, 2.0, 0.5);
    COLOR = vec4(n, n, n, 1.0);
}
```

## Adding a new shader

1. Create `shaders/{name}.gdshader` in your mod
2. If it depends on shared functions, create the `.gdshaderinclude` file
3. Reference it in the Godot scene or from C# code
4. If replacing an existing shader, use the same filename — the mod's version takes priority

===== DOC docs/mods/skills/index.md: Skills =====
Skills are card-based abilities that characters can acquire, equip, and use in battle. Defined as JSON data and rendered as skill cards in the UI.

## JSON structure

```jsonc
{
  // Unique skill ID — used for lookups and save data
  "Id": "slave_master",
  // Locale key for the skill's display name on the card
  "Name": "skills/slave_master/name",
  // Locale key for the skill's tooltip/description text
  "Description": "skills/slave_master/desc",
  // Path to card artwork sprite, resolved from the characters/skills mod group
  "Image": "slave_master",
  // Skill activation type: "Active", "Passive", or "ForCharacter"
  "Type": "Passive",
  // Rarity tier: "Common", "Uncommon", "Rare", "Epic", or "Legendary"
  "Rare": "Legendary",
  // Optional: action card granted to the player when the skill is acquired
  // (active skills hand over a card this way)
  "GrantsAction": "physics/attack",
  // Optional: numeric stat bonuses while the skill is owned
  "Effects": [
    { "Key": "characters/skills/effects/recruit", "Value": 10, "Percent": true }
  ]
}
```

## Field reference

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `Id` | string | Unique skill ID used for lookups and save data |
| `Name` | string | Locale key for display name |
| `Description` | string | Locale key for tooltip/description text |
| `Image` | string | Path to skill card artwork, resolved from mod group `characters/skills` |
| `Type` | string | `"Active"`, `"Passive"`, or `"ForCharacter"` |
| `Rare` | string | `"Common"`, `"Uncommon"`, `"Rare"`, `"Epic"`, or `"Legendary"` |
| `GrantsAction` | string | Optional action card ID pushed into the action pool when the skill is granted |
| `Effects` | array of objects | Optional numeric bonuses granted while the skill is owned — see below |

## Skill types

### `Active`
Must be intentionally triggered during the player's turn, consuming an action resource and targeting a specific ally or enemy. Active skills appear as cards in the player's hand.

### `Passive`
Remains in effect continuously once acquired. Provides a persistent buff or aura without requiring player input. Passive skills do not appear in the hand — they are always active.

### `ForCharacter`
Applies directly to the owning character as a stat modifier or intrinsic trait. These are character-specific upgrades that modify base stats.

### `Effects` — collection bonuses

Each effect is one numeric bonus: `Key` is the locale key naming the affected
stat (e.g. `characters/skills/effects/recruit`), `Value` is the magnitude
(negative values are penalties), and `Percent` selects the display unit
(`+10%` vs `+10`). Effects sharing a `Key` are summed across every owned
skill by `SkillEffectAggregator` into the skills panel's global "collection
effect" plate — so one key must keep the same unit across all skills. The
reference definition `cthangover_game/characters/skills/slave_master.json`
ships one effect: `+10%` recruitment (`"Value": 10, "Percent": true`).

## Granting action cards

An active skill hands the player an action card instead of acting by itself: set `GrantsAction` to the action's `Id`. When the skill is granted, `SkillService` adds the skill to the owned set and appends the card to the action pool, where it can be assigned to a character in the character panel. The skills panel shows the pairing — opening the skill displays "grants an action card" with the card's icon and name.

Granting is idempotent: an already owned skill is ignored, so replaying a scenario event cannot duplicate the card. Action cards awarded this way are not destroyed by the character panel's reset button — reset restores template assignments and returns awarded cards to the pool.

## Rarity tiers

| Tier | Description |
|---|---|
| `Common` | Standard skill — highest drop rate, baseline power |
| `Uncommon` | Slightly improved stats, lower drop rate |
| `Rare` | Notably stronger, appears infrequently |
| `Epic` | Powerful skill with significant gameplay impact, very low drop chance |
| `Legendary` | Game-changing abilities, extremely rare acquisition |

The rarity tier drives the visual frame colour on the skill card via `SkillCardFrameBehaviour`.

## Skill data at runtime

Skill ownership is tracked in `SkillData`:

| Method | Description |
|---|---|
| `HasSkill(id)` | Check if the player owns a skill |
| `AddSkill(id)` | Add a skill to the player's collection (no card grants — prefer `SkillService`) |

Acquisitions go through `SkillService.GrantSkill(id)`, which pairs the skill with its `GrantsAction` card. From a scenario:

```scenario
action character.add_skill skill_id=slave_master
```

The scenario action rejects unknown skill IDs with a log warning and does nothing when the skill is already owned.

## Skill file format

Skill files are self-describing envelopes routed by `Kind`, with no index file:

```jsonc
{
  // Skills are a separate entity kind, not part of characters
  "Kind": "skill",
  "Items": [
    {
      "Id": "slave_master",
      "Name": "characters/skills/slave_master_name",
      "Description": "characters/skills/slave_master",
      "Image": "characters/skills/slave_master",
      "Type": 1,
      "Rare": 3
    }
  ]
}
```

A mod may place this file anywhere; the entity resolver finds it by kind.

## Locale keys

```properties
skills/slave_master/name = Slave Master
skills/slave_master/desc = Increases recruitment chance against defeated enemies by 25%
```

## UI components

| Class | Description |
|---|---|
| `SkillCardBehaviour` | Individual skill card rendering |
| `SkillCardFrameBehaviour` | Card frame with rarity-based colouring |
| `SkillWidget` | Compact skill display widget |
| `SkillsPanelBehaviour` | Skills management panel in game UI |

===== DOC docs/mods/sounds/index.md: Sounds — Sound Effects =====
One-shot sound effect files (`.ogg` / `.wav`) organized by category, referenced from scenarios via the `sound` command.

## Directory structure

```filestree
sounds/
├── battle/           # combat sounds
│   ├── hit.ogg
│   ├── damage.ogg
│   └── stun.ogg
├── locations/        # ambient location sounds
│   ├── door_open.ogg
│   └── fire_crackle.ogg
├── ui/               # interface feedback
│   ├── click.ogg
│   ├── hover.ogg
│   └── notification.ogg
└── record_scratch.ogg
```

## File format

- `.ogg` (Vorbis) and `.wav` are both accepted — see `audio_extensions` in `config/mod_config.json`
- Short duration (0.1–5 seconds) recommended for one-shot effects
- The sound id is the path relative to the mod's `sounds/` folder, minus extension: `sounds/ui/click.ogg` → `ui/click`, `battle/hit`

## Real example — `cthangover_game/sounds/`

From `mods/cthangover_game/sounds/`:

```filestree
sounds/
├── record_scratch.ogg         # comedic timing effect
├── battle/
│   ├── physics_damage.ogg
│   ├── physics_defence.ogg
│   ├── death.ogg
│   └── stun_apply.ogg
├── locations/
│   ├── door_creak.ogg
│   └── wind_ambient.ogg
└── ui/
    ├── button_click.ogg
    ├── notification_chime.ogg
    └── quest_update.ogg
```

## Real example — `card_battle/sounds/`

From `mods/card_battle/sounds/`:

```filestree
sounds/
├── card_pick.ogg              # picking up a card
├── card_drop.ogg              # placing a card on target
├── card_damage.ogg            # damage number appears
├── card_stun.ogg              # stun effect applied
├── card_death.ogg             # card death dissolve
├── turn_start.ogg             # new turn
└── turn_end.ogg               # end turn button
```

## Usage in scenarios

```scenario
sound ui/click
text "- ..." key=dialogue_line
sound battle/hit
delay 0.5
sound ui/notification_chime
```

- `sound` plays immediately and does not block script execution
- Multiple sounds can play simultaneously
- The engine manages audio channels and prevents clipping

## Usage from C#

Sounds are played through `Cthangover.Core.Audio.IAudioService` (implemented by `AudioService`):

```csharp
var audio = GetNodeOrNull<AudioService>("/root/AudioService");

// Exact id
audio.PlaySound("ui/click", SoundType.UI);

// With random variations: picks "battle/hit_1" .. "battle/hit_3"
audio.PlaySound("battle/hit", 3, SoundType.Timed);
```

`SoundType` selects the playback pool — each type gets its own player, so a new
`CardEffect` sound cuts the previous one while a `UI` sound plays independently:
`Timed`, `Background`, `Foreground`, `UI`, `Notification`, `CardEffect`, `CardAction`.

### Engine internals

A few extra members on `AudioService` are useful for mod code:

- `IsMusicPlaying()` — returns `true` when the dedicated Music player is
  currently producing audio; lets UI display playback state without touching
  the player node directly
- `static LinearToDb(float linear)` — public helper converting a linear
  amplitude (0–1) to decibels via `20 * log10(linear)`, clamped to −80 dB at
  the bottom so zero never becomes `-infinity`
- `MixerType` enum (`Sounds`, `Musics`, `Ambient`) — identifies the three audio
  buses (SFX / Music / Ambient) used by `SetVolume(MixerType, float)` and
  `SetEnabled(MixerType, bool)` for per-bus volume and mute control

## Adding a new sound

1. Place the `.ogg` or `.wav` file in `sounds/{category}/`
2. Reference in scenario: `sound category/filename` (no extension)
3. No config or registration needed — audio files are discovered at load time

## Sound categories

Categories are organizational, not enforced. Use whatever folder structure fits your mod:

| Category | Typical use |
|---|---|
| `battle/` | Combat sounds: hits, blocks, stuns, death |
| `locations/` | Environmental: doors, water, wind, fire |
| `ui/` | Interface feedback: clicks, hovers, notifications |
| `music/` | Note: music tracks go in `music/`, not `sounds/` |

For music / ambient tracks, use `music/` and the `music`/`music_play`/`music_pause` scenario commands instead. See `musics/` documentation.

===== DOC docs/mods/src/index.md: C# Source Code in Mods =====
Mods can include C# source files that are compiled at runtime via Roslyn. This allows mods to add new game mechanics, UI, battle systems, and scenario actions without modifying the kernel.

## How it works

1. Kernel discovers `.cs` files via `manifest.json` → `"sources": ["src/**/*.cs"]`
2. Files are loaded as text, compiled via Roslyn into a separate assembly
3. The assembly is loaded into the running game
4. Types implementing known interfaces (`IModInitializer`, `IBattleCore`, `IScenarioAction`, etc.) are auto-discovered

## What gets auto-registered from your assembly

Step 4 happens in `ModAssemblyLoader.RegisterAssembly` (`Core/Mods/ModAssemblyLoader.cs`) — the single point through which every new assembly enters the game. It fans out into every reflection-driven registry, so a public class implementing any of these is discovered without registration code:

| Registry | Discovers |
|---|---|
| `ScenarioActionFactory` | `IScenarioAction` implementations — new `action` commands for the scenario DSL |
| `ScenarioCommandStrategyFactory` | `IScenarioCommandStrategy` implementations, indexed by `Command` name |
| `SceneEventRegistry` (`Core/Scenes/`) | Classes decorated with `[SceneEvent]` deriving from `ExecutableEvent` |
| `ItemActionFactory` | `IItemAction` implementations — item on-use behaviours, indexed by ID |
| `ToolFactory` | `IToolProvider` implementations — tools that add launcher buttons to the main menu's tool block |
| `ToolBoxButtonFactory` | `IToolBoxButton` implementations — toolbox UI buttons |
| `ModInitializerRegistry` | `IModInitializer` mod entry points |
| `ModSaveableRegistry` | `IModSaveable` implementations participating in save/load |
| `ModSettingsRegistry` | `IModSettings` implementations exposed in the settings menu |
| `ModDiscoveryRegistry` | Custom discovery scanners (new places mods can be found) |
| `SectionParserRegistry` | `.tscn` section parsers |
| `PropertyResolverRegistry` | Property resolvers used while building scenes from `.tscn` |
| `SubResourceBuilderRegistry` | Sub-resource builders for `.tscn` construction |
| `ExtResourceBuilderRegistry` | External-resource builders for `.tscn` construction |
| `ModManager.RegisterResolversFromAssembly` | `IModAssetResolver` implementations |

Adding a new reflection-based plugin system to the kernel requires exactly one line in this method. Note that `ScriptTypeRegistry` (below) is populated separately — by `ModRegistry.FinalizeGodotInit`, which calls `ScriptTypeRegistry.RegisterAssembly` right after registering each compiled assembly through `ModAssemblyLoader`.

Subsystems that now live in mods register their own implementations through an `IModInitializer` instead of this fan-out: battle cores and winground listeners (`turn_battle_base`) and recruit behaviours/conditions (`recruiting`). `ModEventBus` typed events with `[EventKey]` can also be declared in any mod — the GDScript bridge reads the attribute at publish time.

## ScriptTypeRegistry — resolving `.tscn` script paths

When a scene file references a script (`path="..."` in its headers), `ScriptTypeRegistry` (`Core/Mods/ScriptTypeRegistry.cs`) maps that path to the compiled `System.Type` inside the mod assembly, respecting mod load-order priority. It is populated by `ModCompiler` after each mod's C# compiles; `ModRegistry.Initialize` supplies the priority order via `SetModOrder`.

Only non-abstract classes deriving from `Godot.Node` are indexed — by class name, full name, and source file path. Paths are normalized before lookup (`res://` prefix stripped, backslashes → forward slashes) and comparisons are case-insensitive.

API (all static):

| Method | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `SetModOrder(List<(string ModId, int Priority)>)` | Sets the mod priority order used during resolution |
| `RegisterAssembly(assembly, modId, sourceFiles)` | Indexes the assembly's node classes and maps source file paths to matching class names |
| `ResolveByPath(tscnPath)` | Priority-ordered lookup by normalized path — first match wins |
| `ResolveByName(className)` | Priority-ordered lookup by class name / full name, with a case-insensitive fallback scan |
| `Resolve(tscnPath)` | Input without `/`, `\` or `.` → name lookup; otherwise path first, then file-name → class-name fallback |
| `Clear()` | Wipes all mappings (called on mod reload) |

## Mod entry point — `IModInitializer`

Mods that need lifecycle logic implement this:

```csharp
public interface IModInitializer
{
    void OnModLoaded(string modId);
    void OnModResourcesReady();
}
```

Implementations are discovered optionally via reflection when the compiled assembly is registered (`ModInitializerRegistry.RegisterAssembly`). Mods that only ship data (JSON scenarios, items, wrappers, etc.) work without any initializer.

## Real example — Card Battle Core

From `mods/card_battle/src/CardBattleCore.cs` (excerpt):

```csharp
namespace Cthangover.CardBattle
{
    public class CardBattleCore : IBattleCore
    {
        public string Id => "card_battle";

        public IActionExecutorProvider ActionProvider { get; }
            = new Actions.CardBattleActionProvider();

        public void Init(Character[] playerChars, Character[] enemyChars,
                         IBattleContext ctx)
        {
            _playerChars = playerChars;
            _ctx = ctx;

            // Deep-copy enemies so defeats are per-instance
            _enemyChars = new Character[enemyChars.Length];
            for (int i = 0; i < enemyChars.Length; i++)
```
